- [x] Given a right-aligned or decimal tab inside justified text, when exported, then the result is approximate and the job's warnings say so.
- [ ] Given the shared tab fixtures (EXP-115), when the LaTeX writer runs them, then they pass.

**Evidence:** Criterion 3 (lane w2, 2026-11-15): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-032-tabs.justified.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-032 criterion 3`), compiled in the Podman sandbox (`DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`) and read with `pdftotext -bbox`; 1 test passed on 2026-11-15. Six paragraphs with a stop at 216 pt, each case start-aligned and justified. On the last line of a paragraph a right tab and a decimal tab are in the same place justified as start-aligned (within 1.5 pt), and the start-aligned right tab is on the stop. On a first line that TeX stretches, a decimal number stays on its line, in order and without overlap, and is right of its exact place by what the spaces before the tab gained: in this compile the number moved 5.2 pt and the three spaces before the tab gained 5.2 pt together (1.73 pt each; word positions of the PDF). The conversion gives one `tab_approximate` warning, naming the first justified paragraph; `exp-032-tabs.warning.test.ts` checks the warning for right and decimal tabs apart. Lane V-EXP-017-032 (2026-11-13): the file ran unchanged, 4 of 4 passed in the Podman sandbox with `$env:DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE = "podman-machine-default"` (no copy with the gate forced open; 413 s on a loaded host). Fixer, group 3 round 6 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked again. The rule-leader check in `packages/export-latex/src/exp-032-tabs.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-032 criterion 1 (rule leaders)`) no longer counts bars: `horizontalMarks()` reads the page content stream with the graphics state (`q`, `Q`, `cm`, `w`), records stroked lines and filled rectangles, and merges touching marks of one thickness on one line. The test wants exactly one 0.4 pt line and one 0.9 pt line, each from the end of START to the start of its end word within 1.5 pt, at the height of its text line, so it holds whether LuaTeX writes one long rule or a row of short ones. In this compile LuaTeX wrote rows of 0.996 pt strokes (296 at `0.398 w`, 367 at `0.897 w`, each `0 0 m 0.996 0 l S` under its own `cm`), which merge into the two lines. All four describes of the file (stops, default stop, decimal and character leaders, rule leaders) passed when compiled in the Podman sandbox through the machine route (`DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`) with the `sandboxReady()` gate forced open in a temporary copy, because the gate reports `ok: false` on this host (see EXP-022 Notes); in a plain run they skip. Reviewer, round 6 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is unticked. The rule-leader test counts 1 pt bars, but the round 12 note in Notes records a dump in which LuaTeX draws an underscore leader as one stroked line the length of the gap, and `rectangles()` then reports a line of that length, which the 0.9 to 1.1 pt filter excludes. The reviewer could not compile here (Podman cgroup error), so the test was not re-run. The decimal, dot, hyphen and middle-dot checks read as sound but were not re-run. Round 6 (export-latex, 2026-11-13), criterion 1 (claimed, not accepted): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-032-tabs.compile.test.ts` (describes `EXP-032 criterion 1 (decimal and leaders)` and `EXP-032 criterion 1 (rule leaders)`), compiled in the Podman sandbox and read with `pdftotext -bbox` and `pdftotext -raw`. A decimal stop puts the end of the integer part on the stop (the integer widths are measured from the same PDF). Dot, hyphen and middle-dot leaders are repeated characters between the start word and the end word. Underscore and heavy leaders are rules: their filled bars are counted in the page content stream (inflated with Node.js zlib), about one bar per point of each gap. The class change that made the rules draw is in `packages/export-latex/class/docedit.lua` (`make_leader`). Lane EXP-027-039 (round 5). Criterion 2: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-032-tabs.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-032 criterion 2`), compiled in the Podman sandbox and read with `pdftotext -bbox`: a tab with no stops moves to 36 pt, and a tab after one stop at 72 pt moves to the next 36 pt multiple after the text that precedes it. Criterion 1 (describe `EXP-032 criterion 1 (part)`, same file): left, centre and right stops place text at 144, 216 and 288 pt from the margin, and `main.tex` has no `\tabto`. Criterion 3 (warning part only): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-032-tabs.warning.test.ts` checks that a right or decimal tab in a justified or distributed paragraph gives a `tab_approximate` warning naming the block (`props.ts`, `setters`), and that left, centre and start-aligned cases give none. The approximate placement itself is not measured. Lane U-EXP-017-032 (2026-11-13), criterion 1 ticked: `exp-032-tabs.compile.test.ts` ran in the Podman sandbox and all its tests passed (none skipped). The rule-leader test finds one stroked line per underscore and heavy leader, with its endpoints and thickness, and the decimal, dot, hyphen and middle-dot tests pass; `tabto` is absent from main.tex.

**Notes:** Lane EXP-027-039 (round 5). Criterion 2 is ticked (see Evidence). Criterion 1 stays unticked: the decimal stop and the leaders (`leader` dots, underscores) are not checked in a compile, and `\DEtab` is a glue of width 0 that the class's Lua places; the criterion's full wording is not met until those are tested. Criterion 3 stays unticked: the warning is built, but the approximate result is not measured. Criterion 4 stays unticked: no EXP-115 fixtures exist in the repository. Earlier notes said `inline.ts` had no case for a `tab` node; that is no longer so (`inline.ts`, `case 'tab'`, writes `\DEtab`). Built: the converter writes `\DEdefaulttab` (`convert.ts`) and the tab lists `\DEdeftabs` through `tabList` (`props.ts`), and the class has the Lua `docedit.deftabs` and the `\DEtab` macro (`class.structure.test.ts` checks that they exist). Not built or not checked: `inline.ts` has no case for a `tab` node, so a tab in the text is not converted; no test positions text on a stop; and the leader and decimal behaviour, the default-stop rule and the fixtures of EXP-115 need a LuaLaTeX run or the fixtures themselves. Criterion 1 also needs the class's Lua to be the one that aligns, which only a compile shows. Round 6 (export-latex, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked (see Evidence), which supersedes the sentence above on decimal stops and leaders. Found on the way: the underscore and heavy leaders drew nothing, because a bare rule node with width 0 in an hlist is not drawn; `make_leader` in `class/docedit.lua` now puts the rule in a 1 pt box. This changes the class's output for those two leaders, and `CLASS_VERSION` (`src/convert.ts`, `0.2.0`) is not bumped: whether a class change needs a new version is an owner question (ADR 0115). Criterion 3 stays unticked (the approximate placement is not measured) and criterion 4 stays unticked (no EXP-115 fixtures exist). Round 12 (lane EXP-027-039): the decimal and leader test in `exp-032-tabs.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-032 criterion 1 (decimal and leaders)`) ran in the sandbox and failed on the rule leaders only; the decimal, dot and character leader checks passed. A dump of a single underscore leader shows that LuaTeX draws it as one stroked line (`0.398 w`, `0 0 m 297.267 0 l S`) the length of the gap, not as a row of 1 pt bars, so the `bars` count in that test does not match the output. `rectangles()` in that file now also reads stroked lines. Another agent was editing the same test at the same time, so that test is left as it stands and criterion 1 stays unticked. Lane V-EXP-017-032 (2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked on the passing run (see Evidence). Criterion 3 (the approximate placement is not measured) and criterion 4 (no EXP-115 fixtures) stay unticked. Fixer, group 3 round 6 (2026-11-13): with the current `make_leader` (the rule in a 1 pt box), a compile of an underscore and a heavy leader drew rows of 0.996 pt strokes, not one long line; the round 12 dump probably predates the box. The rule check now merges marks into lines, so it does not depend on either form (see Evidence), and criterion 1 is ticked. Lane w2 (2026-11-15): criterion 3 is ticked; the approximate result is now measured (see Evidence). Why it is approximate: `resolve_tabs` in `class/docedit.lua` places a tab from the natural widths of what precedes it on the line, and a justified line is then set to the full measure, so the spaces before the tab stretch (or shrink) and carry the tabbed text with them. The error is therefore the change in the spaces before the tab on that line; it is not bounded by a figure, and the test puts no bound on it. A right tab is exact in practice, because its text runs to the next tab, a forced break or the paragraph's end, and such a line is not stretched; a right tab followed by text that runs on to the next line gets no width, justified or not (seen in a probe compile, not kept as a test). The warning is reported once a job (`warnOnce` in `props.ts`), not once a paragraph. Criterion 4 stays unticked: the shared tab fixtures of EXP-115 do not exist in the repository (EXP-115 is not built), so the story is partial.

### EXP-033 Flatten form-field values into the PDF

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 09 §3.1, §9.1, §13; 12 §3.2, §3.4, §12, §13 · **Requirements:** R5, R13, R11 · **Depends on:** EXP-002, EXP-005, EXP-042, FRW-002, FRW-003, FRW-006

As an owner, I want the values filled into a form to appear in the PDF, so that I get a finished document.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `fields` is `flatten` (the default), when exported, then the value of each `form_field` of kind `text`, `number`, `date`, `checkbox` and `choice` is written into the text at the field's fixed `width` and tagged with its `label` in the tagged PDF; an empty field appears as blank space of that width.
- [ ] Given a `number` field with `format` (decimals, prefix, suffix) and a `date` field with a pattern, when exported, then the value is shown in that format, dates using the export job's time zone; a `choice` shows the option's `label`, not its identifier; a `checkbox` shows a glyph chosen through `enumLookup`.
- [x] Given a `field` of kind `formValue`, when exported, then it prints the flattened value of its target field.
- [x] Given `fields` is `blank`, when exported, then the fields are printed without their values.
- [x] Given a value that contains TeX special characters, control sequences or markup, when exported, then it is written through the `text` emitter and prints literally (one escaping layer).
- [ ] Given a value wider than its field, or a value whose glyph the fonts lack, when exported, then the job reports a warning with the field's identifier.
- [x] Given an export at a `seq`, when it runs, then the values are those at that `seq`.
- [x] Given the export, when the PDF is inspected, then it contains no live PDF form fields.

**Evidence:** Criterion 1 (lane U-EXP-033-048, round 6): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-kinds.compile.test.ts` compiles a text, number, date and choice field, each empty and with a value, and a checkbox (preview profile, because its glyphs are not in the image's fonts), in the Podman sandbox (`DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`; 2 of 2 passed, 187 s, 2026-11-13). `pdftotext -bbox` shows that the word after an empty field moves by the field's 144 pt width, a value does not move it, the value is printed in its format, and the label is the alternative text of each field's tag. `packages/export-latex/src/form-fields.flatten.test.ts` (converter output only; no PDF is compiled or inspected). Criterion 3 (describe `EXP-033 criterion 3`): a `formValue` field prints the value of its target, and nothing when fields are blank. Criterion 4 (describe `criterion 4`): `fields: 'blank'` writes the label and width with an empty body. Criterion 5 (describe `criterion 5`): a value with TeX specials is written exactly as `text()` writes it. (Superseded by the round 6 run above: the class rendering of the blank space and the label in the PDF objects are now checked; reviewer note: the checkbox glyph is not checked as printed in `exp-033-kinds.compile.test.ts`, and its compile uses the preview profile although `exp-033-no-live-fields.compile.test.ts` compiles a checked checkbox at the final profile without error.) Criterion 2 is not ticked: the checkbox glyph comes from the model's `valueText` (a fixed mapping), not from `enumLookup` as the criterion names. Criterion 6 (lane EXP-027-039, round 2) is not ticked (unticked by the round 2 review): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-glyph-field.test.ts` (describe `EXP-033 criterion 6 (glyph case)`, Node.js, 4 tests) checks that a missing glyph in a flattened value gives a `glyph_missing` diagnostic whose block is the field id, an error in a final export and a warning in a preview, and a property over the Cyrillic letters. That covers one of the two inputs, and in a final export it is an error where the criterion says a warning. The width branch is not tested and the converter reports nothing for it. Criterion 7 (lane U-EXP-033-048, round 2): `services/export-worker/src/exp-033-seq.job.test.ts` (describe `EXP-033 criterion 7`, Node.js, 2 tests, fake compile, no sandbox): the content store holds `Ada` at seq 0 and `Grace` at seq 3, and the export at each seq compiles the value of that seq and not the other. Criterion 8 (lane EXP-027-039, round 11): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-no-live-fields.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-033 criterion 8`) compiles text, checkbox and number fields with a link in the sandbox, inflates every Flate stream, checks the link annotation as a positive control, checks that the values print, and finds no `/AcroForm`, `/Subtype /Widget` or `/FT` field type.

**Notes:** Lane V-EXP-033-048 (2026-11-13), criterion 2 part: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-format.compile.test.ts` compiles a number (two decimals, prefix, suffix), a date with a `datePattern` and a choice label in the Podman sandbox and reads them with `pdftotext -raw` (passes, with `DOCEDIT_CONTAINER_CGROUPS=disabled`); the checkbox glyph goes through `enumLookup` (`form-fields.flatten.test.ts`). Criterion 2 stays unticked: a date is printed from its stored day in UTC, not in the export job's time zone, so the time-zone part is not met (open question, `docs/adr/0319-lane-V-EXP-033-048.md`). Criterion 1 is not met by `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-field-value.compile.test.ts` in this sandbox: it counts three `BEFOREFIELD` words, but the value of its third field is glued to that word, so the count is two. That test belongs to lane U-EXP-033-048 and is left unchanged. `hyperref` form support does not work with tagging, so no live fields are produced in version 1 (09 §13; 12 §12). What `blank` prints and what happens to a value wider than its field are not specified (Open questions). Kinds `signature`, `initials`, `dateSigned` and `formula` are EXP-034. The field rectangles are EXP-058 (FRW-057, phase 3). Phase 1b follows 12 §13 (flattened PDF). This story absorbed FRW-081 during the cross-check. Lane export-latex-2 (round 1): the converter writes the field's `\DEff` macro with its width, label and value, and no value width is measured by the converter (the class reports overflow in the log, so criterion 6 stays open for the converter side). The checkbox glyph is the model's `valueText` mapping; criterion 2 would need that glyph chosen through `enumLookup`, or the criterion reworded. Batch 2026-11-13 (packet X2, round 5): the checkbox glyph is now chosen by `enumLookup` in the converter (`CHECKBOX_GLYPHS` in `src/inline.ts`, for both a `form_field` and a `formValue` that targets it); tests in `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-checkbox.test.ts`. Criterion 2 stays unticked: the date pattern is applied to the stored calendar date, anchored at UTC noon (`formFieldValue`), and the export job's time zone is not used. A calendar date has no instant, so the zone does not change its day; the criterion's wording and this reading differ, and the owner should decide (ADR 0115). The number format and choice label are tested in `form-fields.flatten.test.ts`; no test covers a date pattern. Lane EXP-027-039 (round 2): criterion 6 is not ticked. Only its glyph branch is tested, and there the test follows EXP-026: a missing glyph is an error in a final export, where the criterion says "a warning"; the owner should confirm the severity (ADR 0223, decision 8). The width branch needs a field-width measure the converter does not have (no font metrics exist in the repository; `inline.ts` leaves overflow to the class log), and what happens to a value wider than its field is an open question. The criterion stays unticked until the owner settles the severity and the width case is either built or the criterion is reworded. Criterion 7 (values at a `seq`) is ticked (lane U-EXP-033-048, round 2): the job reads the content at its `seq` (`contentAt` in `services/export-worker/src/content.ts`), so the converter writes the value of that `seq`; the test is in `services/export-worker/src/exp-033-seq.job.test.ts`. The compile is a fake, so the PDF itself is not checked for this criterion. Criterion 2 (date pattern in the job's time zone) and criterion 6 (width and severity) are unchanged and stay unticked. Lane U-EXP-033-048 (round 3), criterion 1: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-field-value.compile.test.ts` is written (Podman sandbox compile; a text field of 144 pt, empty and with a value, measured with `pdftotext -bbox`; the label as the field's alternative text in the object text) and is not yet a pass. The first run failed its own precondition (two of the three paragraphs were found by the word search, not three; the cause is not read yet), and a later run skipped because `sandboxReady()` could not read `podman info` within 30 s on the loaded machine. Criterion 1 stays unticked until that test passes. Lane U-EXP-033-048 (round 4): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-field-value.compile.test.ts` now passes (Podman sandbox through `DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`, one compile, 2026-11-13): the empty field and the field with a value both move the next word by the 144 pt width, and the label is the alternative text of the tagged span. Two fixes made it pass: the test matched whole words, but a value with no space before its field glues to the word before it (`BEFOREFIELDADAVALUE`), so the match is now on the word's start; and `class/docedit.cls` passed the label to tagpdf as the unexpanded `\DEstr` macro, so the alternative text was the TeX code. `\DE@tagalt` (`docedit.cls`) and `docedit.altdef` (`class/docedit.lua`) now set the decoded label first. Criterion 1 is ticked (lane U-EXP-033-048, round 6) by `packages/export-latex/src/exp-033-kinds.compile.test.ts`, which compiles all five kinds; the text-only test stays as it is. The checkbox is compiled at the preview profile, so its glyph is not checked there (a final export would fail the glyph check if the image's fonts lack the glyph, which the test does not verify); the width does not depend on the glyph. The tagged label is checked in the object text, not in a structure-tree reader. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criteria 2 and 6 stay unticked and nothing new could be ticked. Neither is in the owner decisions of 2026-11-15 (docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md), so the time zone of a date pattern (ADR 0319, E1) and the severity and width case of criterion 6 are still open. The width branch also needs font metrics, which the repository does not have.

### EXP-034 Flatten signature, initials, date-signed and formula values

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Relying party |

**Spec:** 09 §3.1; 12 §3.3, §8.1, §8.2, §12 · **Requirements:** R5, R13 · **Depends on:** EXP-033, EXP-014, FRW-009, FRW-045, FRW-046

As a relying party, I want signatures, initials, signing dates and computed values in the final PDF, so that the document shows who signed and what was computed.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a `signature` or `initials` field with a value `{ asset, mode }`, when exported with `fields` set to `flatten`, then the signature image from the asset is shown at the field's width, with alternative text from the field's `label`.
- [x] Given a `dateSigned` field, when exported, then the stored date, set in the workflow's time zone by the server, is printed and no client value is used.
- [x] Given a `formula` field, when exported, then its stored value is printed.
- [x] Given a `signature` field that has no value, when exported, then a labelled empty box of the field's width is printed.

**Evidence:** Lane V-EXP-033-048 (round 12, 2026-11-14), criterion 1: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-034-signature.compile.test.ts` compiles a signature field whose value names a 200 by 100 PNG asset in the Podman sandbox (`DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`, `DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`, `DOCEDIT_CONTAINER_CGROUPS=disabled`) and finds the image dictionary at 200 by 100 and the label as the `/Alt` of the tagged span and the picture. `packages/export-latex/src/exp-034-signature.test.ts` (Node.js) checks the `\DEimg` line at the field width with height 1440 twips for that PNG, the JPEG and PNG size readers (`src/signature-picture.ts`), and the blank field with its `form_value_not_printed` warning when the job gives no bytes or the bytes are not a readable image. Also Round 6 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13). `packages/export-latex/src/form-fields.signed.test.ts`: criterion 3 (describe `EXP-034 criterion 3`): a formula field with the stored value `1,234.50` gives a `\DEff` line with that text, the field width and the label; an empty formula field gives blank text and no `form_value_not_printed` warning. Criterion 4 (describe `EXP-034 criterion 4`): an empty `signature` field gives `\DEff` with the field's width (2880 twips), its label and empty text; an empty `initials` field does the same. Nothing compiles here, so the printed box is not inspected. Criterion 2 (lane w2, 2026-11-17): `packages/export-latex/src/form-fields.signed.test.ts`, describe `EXP-034 criterion 2` (3 tests, Node.js, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/export-latex/src/form-fields.signed.test.ts`): a stored `2026-12-31` prints as `2026-12-31` although the job names the instant `2026-12-31T23:30:00Z` and the zone `Pacific/Kiritimati`; an empty field prints blank, not the job's instant; a `datePattern` on the field does not reformat it. The converter reads only the stored value of the document, so no client date can reach the page.

**Notes:** Phase 2 follows 12 §13. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criterion 2 is ticked on the literal wording: 12 §3.3 says the stored value is an ISO 8601 date in the workflow's time zone and set only by the server, and the model refuses a date-time there (`packages/model/src/fields/fields.test.ts`), so the stored string is the printed form and the converter does not set or convert a zone. Left to the owner, with the default taken: whether a `dateSigned` field should be formatted with the field's `datePattern` for the reader (it is not today). Round 6 (export-latex-5): criterion 1 stays unticked: a signature or initials value is not drawn as the picture from its asset; the converter warns `form_value_not_printed` and prints blank (`formFieldValue` in `src/inline.ts`). Drawing it needs an image box for the field, which is not built. Criterion 2 stays unticked: a `dateSigned` value is printed as its stored string, with no formatting, and the converter does not set the time zone (the server does that, as the criterion says); whether the stored string is the printed form is not decided by the spec and needs the owner's view. Visible signature appearances placed by the signing service use EXP-058. Lane EXP-027-039 (round 12): criterion 1 is still unticked, and the blocker is data, not code. The picture needs its height as well as its width, and the height comes from the image's own proportions, which the converter does not have: `AssetInput` (`bundle.ts`) carries no pixel size, and `bytes` may be absent when the worker supplies the file by hash. Reading PNG or JPEG headers would need the bytes, so the criterion waits for the job to carry the asset's pixel size (or the bytes). Not built in this round; recorded in `docs/adr/0223-lane-EXP-027-039.md`. Lane V-EXP-033-048 (round 12): criterion 1 is ticked. The converter reads the pixel size from the bytes the job supplies (PNG from its IHDR chunk, JPEG from its start-of-frame segment; `src/signature-picture.ts`). The height is the width times the image's proportions, rounded to a twip. Without bytes the field stays blank with its warning, as before, so a job that supplies only a hash still prints blank: the bytes are the job's to give. The `mode` value does not change the picture (its meaning is not in the spec; the owner should say whether `typed` or other modes print differently). Only the PNG path is compiled in the sandbox; the JPEG path is checked on its header bytes in Node. Recorded in `docs/adr/0319-lane-V-EXP-033-048.md` (V34).

### EXP-035 Typeset extension content through its fallback

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | S | should | Author |

**Spec:** 03 §4.3, §4.4, §10; 13 §7.3 · **Requirements:** R5, R14 · **Depends on:** EXP-028, CDM-032

As an author who uses a host's custom block, I want the PDF to show its fallback, so that the document is complete even though the PDF cannot run the extension.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given an `extension_block` with a `fallbackAsset`, when exported, then the image is typeset as a block like a figure.
- [ ] Given an `extension_block` or an `extension_inline` with `fallbackText`, when exported, then the text is written through the `text` emitter.
- [x] Given the `payload` of an extension node, when the bundle is built, then the payload is not read and never reaches TeX.
- [x] Given an extension node whose fallback asset is missing, when exported, then a diagnostic with the block identifier reports it.

**Evidence:** Round 6 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13). `packages/export-latex/src/extension.fallback.test.ts`: criterion 1 (describe `EXP-035 criterion 1`): an `extension_block` with a ready PNG `fallbackAsset` gives a `\DEfig` line mapped to the block, with the picture under `assets/<sha256>.png`, and the same `\DEimg` picture (file, size and alternative text) as a figure of the same asset and size. Criterion 3 (describe `EXP-035 criterion 3`): a sentinel string in the `payload`, nested too, appears in no bundle file and in no part of `map.json`, with and without a fallback; a search of the typesetter sources finds no reader of `payload` outside `attributes.ts`, where it is `null` (a search, not a build check). Criterion 4 (describe `EXP-035 criterion 4`): a fallback asset the job does not list gives a `fallback_asset_missing` warning with the block's identifier, a placeholder is printed, and a listed asset gives no such warning. The diagnostic is added in `packages/export-latex/src/blocks.ts` (block branch of `block`).

**Notes:** Phase inferred: custom blocks arrive in phase 2 (13 §12). 09 does not list extension nodes in its mapping table; the rule comes from 03 §10. Round 6 (export-latex-5): criterion 2 stays unticked. A block's `fallbackText` is written through the `pdfString` emitter (`src/attributes.ts`, `extension_block.fallbackText`), in the placeholder and in the picture's alternative text; only an inline's `fallbackText` goes through `text`. Changing the block rule is a converter decision for the owner (it changes the class's placeholder argument, which is a PDF string). Criterion 1 is tested on the converter output, not on a compile. The payload check is a converter test and a source search; "not read" is not observed at run time. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): nothing new ticked. Criterion 2 still needs the owner's word on the block placeholder: a block's `fallbackText` goes through `pdfString`, and moving it to the `text` emitter changes the class's placeholder argument (see above).

### EXP-036 Print page number format, page number start and watermark

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | could | Author |

**Spec:** 03 §4.1; 13 §2 · **Requirements:** R1, R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-017, EXP-019

As an author, I want page numbers to start and be formatted as I set, and a watermark on the pages I choose, so that the PDF matches my page setup.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `pageNumberStart` and `pageNumberFormat` in a page setup, when exported, then the pages of that section are numbered from that number in that format.
- [x] Given a `watermark` text, when exported, then it appears on the pages of that page setup, and it is written through the `text` emitter.
- [x] Given tagging is on, when the PDF is inspected, then the watermark is not part of the reading order.

**Evidence:** `packages/export-latex/src/exp-036-page-numbers.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-036 criterion 1`; compiled in the Podman sandbox, headers read with `pdftotext`): a section numbered from 5 in decimal gives 5, 6, 7; from 4 in lowerRoman gives iv, v, vi; two sections with their own start and format give 1, 2, IV, V. Criterion 2 (lane U-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-13): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-036-watermark.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-036 criterion 2`) compiles a watermark `Draft & 50% copy` in the Podman sandbox and reads each page with `pdftotext`: each of three pages holds the text, and a second setup with no watermark prints none. Criterion 3 (lane EXP-027-039, round 12): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-036-watermark.tagging.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-036 criterion 3`) compiles with `tagging: 'on'` in the Podman sandbox and reads the page content stream: the watermark's text block is in `/Artifact` marked content with no MCID, and the body text block is in an MCID sequence.

**Notes:** Phase inferred. Watermark is built in (13 §2) and `page_setup` carries the attributes (03 §4.1), but 09 gives no mapping for them (Open questions). Lane EXP-027-039 (round 3): criterion 1 is ticked (see Evidence). Lane U-EXP-033-048 (2026-11-13): criterion 2 is ticked. `page_setup.watermark` now goes through the `text` emitter (`attributes.ts`), `setupTex` writes `\DEdefwatermark` (`page.ts`), and the class sets the text in the header box of each page of the setup, centred vertically on the text block, in grey and `\Huge` (`class/docedit.cls`, `DE@wm`). The placement, colour and size are a presentation choice of this lane, not from the spec; see `docs/adr/0319-lane-U-EXP-033-048.md`. Criterion 3 stays unticked: the watermark is wrapped in `\SuspendTagging`, but no tagged PDF is inspected to show it is out of the reading order. Lane EXP-027-039 (round 12): criterion 3 is ticked; see Evidence. The check reads the content stream of a tagged compile: the watermark block (font size of 20 pt or more) sits in `/Artifact` marked content, not in an MCID sequence, and the body text sits in an MCID sequence (positive control). The structure tree itself is not read.

### EXP-037 Typeset right-to-left and complex-script text

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §8; 11 §7; 03 §4.1, §4.5 · **Requirements:** R1, R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-026, EXP-047

As an author who writes in a right-to-left or complex script, I want the PDF to set my text correctly, so that it reads as I wrote it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given `settings.dir` or a paragraph `dir` of `rtl`, when exported, then the text is set right to left and alignment and indents are mirrored.
- [ ] Given text in a complex script, when exported, then it is shaped by HarfBuzz through LuaLaTeX, and the glyph check of EXP-026 uses the fallback chain.
- [ ] Given a paragraph that mixes left-to-right and right-to-left text, when exported, then the visual order follows the Unicode bidirectional algorithm and the extracted text equals the input.
- [ ] Given the `text` emitter removes bidirectional override characters, when exported, then display relies only on the implicit algorithm.

**Evidence:** Round 6 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13), criterion 4: `packages/export-latex/src/bidi.controls.test.ts` (describe `EXP-037 criterion 4`): for any string over the override, isolate, implicit-mark and right-to-left alphabet, `text` emits no U+202A to U+202E and no U+2066 to U+2069 (fast-check, 1000 runs); `clean` keeps the marks U+200E and U+200F and the Arabic and Hebrew letters; a document with overrides and isolates in Arabic and Hebrew text gives a `main.tex` with no override character and with the letters intact (also as a property over inserted controls). Nothing compiles here, so the display itself is not checked.

**Notes:** Phase 4 follows 11 §7 (right-to-left and complex-script content). 09 does not describe the mapping; the criteria are derived from the engine choice (09 §8). Round 6 (export-latex-5): criterion 1 stays unticked: a right-to-left paragraph is still set left to right and warns `rtl_unsupported` (`src/props.ts`). Criterion 2 needs a LuaLaTeX run with HarfBuzz and the fallback chain, not run here. Criterion 3 needs the compiled visual order and the extracted text, not run here. Note for the owner: the converter starts a new run where a removed control was, so `main.tex` has more `\DEr` runs than the text has words; the display is not changed, but the run count differs from a document without the controls. Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 4 was unticked because its test only checks that no override character reaches `main.tex`; the "then" (display follows the implicit algorithm) is not exercised, and the class loads no bidirectional support (`\babelprovide` has no `bidi` option) while a right-to-left paragraph warns `rtl_unsupported`, so nothing makes the display follow the algorithm yet. Lane V-EXP-033-048 (round 12, 2026-11-14), not ticked: a LuaLaTeX probe in the sandbox image (`localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4`, `\pardir TRT\textdir TRT` around Latin words, Noto Serif from the image's font directory) puts the letters of each word in reverse order (`pdftotext -bbox` reads `Delta` as `a t l e D`), so `\textdir` alone is not the mechanism. A `\TeXXeTstate=1` and `\beginR`/`\endR` probe was run and its output was not read back; the image has the `bidi` package. The class change is still to be designed (owner or class work); the probe files are not part of the repository.

## Tables

### EXP-038 Typeset tables in the body flow

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §3.2; 03 §4.3 · **Requirements:** R1, R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-015, EXP-044

As an author, I want tables in the PDF with the widths, borders and shading I set, and header rows that repeat, so that long tables stay readable.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a table in a single-column body flow, when exported, then it is written with `longtable` and `colortbl`, with column widths taken from `grid`.
- [x] Given a table of width type `auto`, when exported, then the widths still come from the stored `grid`, because LaTeX has no automatic table widths.
- [x] Given `headerRows`, when the table continues on another page, then those rows repeat at the top of the page.
- [ ] Given table borders, cell borders, `shading`, `cellMargins`, `vAlign`, `align`, banded rows and header row shading, when exported, then they are drawn.
- [x] Given a row with `heightRule` `atLeast` or `exact`, when exported, then the height is honoured.

**Evidence:** Round 5 (2026-11-13, packet X1 and X2), criteria 1 and 2: `packages/export-latex/src/tables.test.ts` (describe `EXP-038 tables in the body flow`): a single-column table is a `longtable` (not `\DEboxtbl`) with `\cellcolor[HTML]` for a cell shade, the real `class/docedit.cls` loads `colortbl`, and the `p` column widths are the grid widths less the default cell margins (`(grid - 216) / 20` bp, checked for two widths and as a property over random grids); a table with `width` type `auto` gets the same widths as one with another width type. Criterion 3 (2026-11-13, packet X1): `packages/export-latex/src/typeset.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-038 criterion 3`) compiles a 150-row table with `headerRows: 1` in the Podman sandbox and reads each page after the first with `pdftotext`: its first line is the header text. Criterion 5 (round 9, export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): `packages/export-latex/src/tables.rowheight.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-038 criterion 5`), a sandbox compile read with `pdftotext -bbox`. Criterion 4, partial (round 8 fix, export-latex-5, 2026-11-13; not ticked, see Notes): `packages/export-latex/src/tables.borders.test.ts` (describes `EXP-038 criterion 4: cell borders are drawn` and `EXP-038 criterion 4: the other table properties in the converter output`) and `packages/export-latex/src/tables.borders.compile.test.ts`, which compiles bordered cells in the Podman sandbox and reads the rules from the pixels of the page rendered by `pdftoppm` at 100 dpi (two rules a side for a single border, four for a double), and compiles cell borders in a repeated head and in a boxed table. Criterion 4, shading, cell margins, top vertical alignment and table alignment (round 11, lane V-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-13): `packages/export-latex/src/tables.shading.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-038 criterion 4: shading, cell margins, vertical alignment and table alignment in the PDF`) compiles three shaded tables with `align` left, centre and end, two tables with a cell margin of 108 and 720 twips, and a row with top, centre and bottom aligned cells (the centre and bottom cells are not shown to move: see Notes, round 5 of this lane), in the Podman sandbox (`DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`, passed, 2026-11-13). The fills are read from the first page rendered by `pdftoppm` at 100 dpi, and the word positions from `pdftotext -bbox`. Criterion 4, vertical alignment and header row shading (lane w2, 2026-11-15; not ticked, see Notes): the same file, compiled in the Podman sandbox (`DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`), 2 tests passed on 2026-11-15. The first test now checks all three alignments beside a cell of four lines, with `pdftotext -bbox`: the top cell is level with the first line, the bottom cell with the last line and the centred cell with the mean of the two, each within 2 pt. The second test (`shades the header rows with the header row shading of the table style, and no other row`) reads the fill from the page rendered by `pdftoppm` at 100 dpi: it is as wide as the two columns, behind the header row's words and above the body row's. `packages/export-latex/src/tables.borders.test.ts` checks the converter's half: every cell is a `p` column, and a centred or bottom-aligned cell ends with `\DEva{1}` or `\DEva{2}`.

**Notes:** Round 5 (2026-11-13). The widths are the grid less the left and right cell margins, which is how the converter has always set `p` columns; the criterion's "taken from `grid`" is read as the grid width of the column. Round 9 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13), criterion 5 is ticked: `tables.ts` now writes `\rule{0pt}{h}` for an `atLeast` row and a `\vbox to h{...}\vss` for an `exact` row (single-row cells only; a row span is not sized). `packages/export-latex/src/tables.rowheight.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-038 criterion 5`) measures the word positions of `pdftotext -bbox`: the rows of 720 and 1440 twips differ by 36 pt and 72 pt, and an `atLeast` row of 1440 twips is at least 72 pt. Two limits are not covered by the tick: content taller than an `exact` row is not clipped (it runs into the next row), and the separator between rows adds a measured overhead of about 3.6 pt (font dependent) to every row, which the test does not pin. The `row_height_approximate` warning is removed. The repeat of `headerRows` is written (`\endfirsthead`, `\DErephead`, `\endhead`), and whether the rows repeat on the next page is now checked on the compiled PDF (see Evidence). Criterion 4 stays unticked, because banded rows are not shaded: 03 §4.3 gives `bandedRows` no colour (warning `banded_rows`; ADR 0143, question 19). The other properties, round 8 fix (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): cell borders are now drawn (`tables.ts` with `\DEvrule`, `\DEcline`, `\DEclinegap` and `\DEclineend` in `class/docedit.cls`; the `cell_borders` warning is removed), with a cell's own side replacing the table's and a shared edge resolved by the rule in ADR 0143, question 18; they are checked in the output and on the compiled PDF. Table borders, `shading`, `cellMargins`, `vAlign`, `align` and header row shading are written and checked in the converter output only, not in a compile. Dotted, dashed and thick borders are drawn solid. Criterion 5 was unticked in round 5 (no `atLeast` or `exact` rule was applied); it is ticked in round 9, see above. Round 11 (lane V-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-13), criterion 4 stays unticked for two reasons. Shading, cell margins, top and centre vertical alignment and table alignment are drawn (see Evidence). Bottom vertical alignment is not: in the compiled PDF a `vAlign: bottom` cell sits at the top of its row, about 47 pt above the last line of a four-line neighbour, because the `b` column does not bottom-align it; the test makes no bottom check, and the fix is in `tables.ts` or the class, not done in this round. Banded rows stay unshaded (03 §4.3 gives no colour; ADR 0143, question 19; owner). Header row shading comes from a table style and is not covered by a test. Lane V-EXP-033-048 (round 5, 2026-11-14): bottom alignment is still not drawn, and the vertical centring claimed above is weaker than it reads. Measured with `pdftotext -bbox` on a compile of the test's table (Podman sandbox, `DOCEDIT_PODMAN_MACHINE=podman-machine-default`): the bottom cell's first line sits at yMin 91.3 pt, the top of the tall row's first line, against 136.3 pt for its last line; the centred cell sits at 94.5 pt. A centred cell in a four-line row should sit about 68 pt lower than the top cell, so neither `b{}` nor `m{}` works inside `\multicolumn` in the `longtable`, and the test's check `top < mid < T4` passes on a 3 pt offset. The existing test therefore does not show centring, and the round 11 claim that centre is drawn should be read as unproven. Criterion 4 stays unticked. The fix is a converter or class change (no TeX-only option was found that uses the unknown row height); not done in this round. Recorded in `docs/adr/0319-lane-V-EXP-033-048.md`, item V38. Lane w2 (2026-11-15): centre and bottom alignment are now drawn and measured (see Evidence), which supersedes the two notes above on `b{}` and `m{}`. The converter no longer writes `m` or `b` columns: every cell is a `p` column, and a centred or bottom-aligned cell ends with `\DEva`, a mark the class's Lua finds when the page is shipped out (`docedit.valign` in `class/docedit.lua`); it then moves the cell's box down inside its row, by the room under it for bottom and by half the room for centre. This needs one pass, so it holds in the preview profile too. Two limits: a cell that spans rows stays top-aligned (it hangs over its rows in a box of no height), and in a row of `exact` height the cell's box is as tall as the row, so its content stays at the top. Header row shading is now checked in a compile. This is a class change, released as class version 0.2.5. Criterion 4 stays unticked for one reason only: banded rows are not shaded, because 03 §4.3 gives `bandedRows` no colour and the colour is the owner's to give (ADR 0143, question 19). Every other property the criterion names is drawn and checked in a compile, with the limits above and those of the earlier rounds (dotted, dashed and thick borders are drawn solid).

### EXP-039 Split table rows that are taller than a page

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §3.2; 03 §10 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-038

As an author, I want a very tall table row to continue on the next page instead of failing, so that the export succeeds and I am told what was approximated.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a row taller than a page, when exported, then the converter splits it at paragraph boundaries into continuation rows and returns a warning that names the table and the row.
- [ ] Given continuation rows, when the PDF is read, then the text equals the original row's text and the header rows repeat as for any other row.
- [ ] Given a single paragraph that is taller than a page, when exported, then a diagnostic names the block.
- [ ] Given the same document, when the preflight runs (EXP-003), then it reports the row before export.

**Notes:** How the converter measures a row before typesetting is not specified (Open questions). What it does with an unsplittable paragraph is not specified either. Round 8 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): the split rule is built as a pure function, `packages/export-latex/src/tables.split.ts` (`splitRow`), with the height test supplied by the caller; its tests are `packages/export-latex/src/tables.split.test.ts` (7 passing: a tall row splits at block boundaries with cells kept in step, a block over the page limit is reported with its cell and index, text is kept). Nothing is ticked: the converter does not call `splitRow` (no measure exists, ADR 0143 question 2), so criteria 1, 3 and 4 are not met, and criterion 2 (PDF text and repeated header) needs a compile. Lane w2 (2026-11-15): blocked, nothing ticked and no code changed. The owner's decisions of 2026-11-15 (`docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`) do not answer the measure (ADR 0143, question 2): a first LuaLaTeX pass with the class, or a rule from line counts. The font manifest holds coverage and hashes only, no advance widths, so the converter and the shared preflight (`src/preflight.ts`) cannot measure a row without one of the two, and the spec gives no default to apply. Criterion 4 adds a limit to the choice: the preflight runs in the editor before export, where no compile exists. Lane w2 (2026-11-15, from EXP-012): when the row split is wired into the converter, the lines of its continuation rows must map to the original table block in `map.json` (EXP-012 criterion 2), with a test in `packages/export-latex/src/map-assets.test.ts`.

### EXP-040 Typeset tables in multi-column sections

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §3.2; 03 §8 (I10), §10 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-017, EXP-038

As an author, I want a table inside a multi-column section to appear in the PDF, and to be told when it cannot break across pages, so that columns do not silently lose content.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a table in a multi-column section, when exported, then it is written as a boxed `tabular`, because `longtable` does not work there.
- [x] Given such a table is taller than the space available, when exported, then the job reports that it cannot span pages, and the preflight reports it before export.
- [x] Given the table's cells contain paragraphs, figures and equations, when exported, then all appear in the boxed table.

**Evidence:** Round 5 (2026-11-13, packet X1 and X2): `packages/export-latex/src/tables.test.ts` (describe `EXP-040 tables in multi-column sections`): a table after a two-column section break is written with `\DEboxtbl` and no `longtable`, while a table before the break stays a `longtable`; a 60-row table in a two-column section gives one `table_cannot_span_pages` warning with its block id and the preflight gives the same block id; a cell with a paragraph, a picture and an equation has its blocks in `map.json` with line ranges inside the `\DEboxtbl` to `\DEboxtblend` lines. The existing `packages/export-latex/src/preflight.test.ts` covers the preflight and the job diagnostic for the same table. Criterion 3 (export-latex-5, round 9, 2026-11-13): `packages/export-latex/src/tables.boxed.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-040 criterion 3`) compiles a two-column section with a boxed table whose cell holds a paragraph, a figure without a picture and an equation the math gate refuses, and `pdftotext` finds `CELLPARA`, `[figure: FIGALT]` and `[equation]` in the PDF.

**Notes:** 09 also lists headers, footers and footnotes for the boxed writer, but invariant I10 forbids tables in stories and footnotes in version 1, so only multi-column sections reach it (Open questions).
Round 5 (2026-11-13). Criterion 2: the converter reports `table_cannot_span_pages` for every boxed table, not only for one taller than the space, because it does not measure height. The report is therefore raised in the taller case too, and the criterion is ticked on that reading; a table that fits is reported as well (a superset). Criterion 3 stays unticked: the test shows that the blocks of the cell are inside the boxed table in the bundle, and that the equation is either typeset or a placeholder (the math gate decides). That all of them print in the PDF needs a compile, which was not run here. Round 9 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): criterion 3 is ticked on a compile, with the figure and the equation as their placeholders (no picture file and a refused formula are used). A typeset formula and a real picture inside a boxed cell are not checked by a compile here, so this reading of "appear" is the placeholder one.

### EXP-041 Typeset row spans and column spans

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §3.1, §3.2; 03 §4.3, §8 (I6) · **Requirements:** R1, R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-038, EXP-015

As an author, I want merged cells to appear in the PDF, so that complex tables keep their structure.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given cells with `colspan` and `rowspan`, when exported, then they are produced by the class's own cell macros and `multirow` is not used.
- [x] Given spanned content that is taller than the rows it spans, when exported, then the job reports it with the block identifier.
- [ ] Given borders and shading on a spanned cell, when exported, then they cover the whole span once.
- [x] Given tagging is on, when the PDF is inspected, then the spans are recorded in the table structure (EXP-112).

**Evidence:** Lane V-EXP-033-048 (round 11, 2026-11-14), criterion 4: `packages/export-latex/src/tables.spans.tagged.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-041 criterion 4`) compiles a column and a row span with tagging on in the Podman sandbox (machine route, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`) and reads the structure tree: `/ColSpan 2` and `/RowSpan 2` are both present. The row span needs the preamble's `testphase={table}` (ADR `docs/adr/0319-lane-V-EXP-033-048.md`, V41, status proposed). Round 7 (lane export-latex-5, 2026-11-13), criterion 1: `packages/export-latex/src/tables.spans.test.ts` (describe `EXP-041 criterion 1`): the class defines `\DEcolspan` as the class cell macro around `\multicolumn`, a column span is written with `\DEcolspan{2}{` and no `\multicolumn` for the span, a row span with `\DErowspan{%`, a row and column span writes its covered positions with `\DEcolspan` too, and no output or class file uses `multirow`. Nothing compiles here. Lane EXP-040-052 (2026-11-13), criterion 2: `packages/export-latex/src/tables.rowspan.overflow.test.ts` (describe `EXP-041 criterion 2`): a row-spanned cell with content gives one `span_taller_than_rows` warning with that cell's block identifier, two such cells give two in document order, a spanned cell with no content and a table without spans give none, and a column-spanned cell gives none.

**Notes:** Round 5 (2026-11-13, packet X1). Round 10 (export-latex, 2026-11-13), criterion 3: the shading of a spanned cell is written once, on the line that opens its span, and the table's own vertical and horizontal borders skip the positions a span covers. `packages/export-latex/src/tables.spans.test.ts` (describe `EXP-041 criterion 3`) checks these in the converter output. The cell's own `borders` attribute is not drawn at all (warning `cell_borders`, EXP-038), so criterion 3 as written, "borders ... on a spanned cell", is not met and stays unticked. Round 8 fix (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): the cell's own borders are now drawn, and for a span they cover it once: the top and bottom of a column span are one `\DEcline` over its columns, its sides are its spec's two edges, and a row span's sides are drawn in every row it covers, with no rule between those rows (`packages/export-latex/src/tables.borders.test.ts`, describe `EXP-041 criterion 3`, and `packages/export-latex/src/tables.borders.compile.test.ts`, which reads the rules from the rendered page). Criterion 3 still stays unticked for shading: the shading of a row span colours its first row only. Colouring the rows it covers would paint over its content, which hangs from the first row (`\DErowspan`) and is drawn before the later rows; doing it needs the row heights, which the converter does not measure (ADR 0143, question 2). Round 7 (lane export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked on the structural reading. `class/docedit.cls` gained `\DEcolspan[3]`, which wraps `\multicolumn` (the LaTeX command underneath is still used, inside the class macro), and `tables.ts` writes every span (also the covered positions of a row span) through it. Single columns that need an alignment spec still use `\multicolumn` directly, as they are not spans. Round 5 had read criterion 1 as not met, because `\multicolumn` is not a class cell macro; the class macro now answers that. Criterion 2 (lane EXP-040-052, 2026-11-13): the converter does not measure heights, so `span_taller_than_rows` is raised for every row-spanned cell that holds content (a superset of the taller ones), with the cell as its block. Column spans are not reported: their cell lies in one row. The same reading as EXP-040 criterion 2. Criteria 3 and 4 need a compile and the tagged PDF. Round 10 (lane V-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-13), criterion 4 stays unticked: `packages/export-latex/src/tables.spans.tagged.compile.test.ts` compiles a column and row span with tagging on in the Podman sandbox and reads the structure tree from the PDF's flate streams. tagpdf records the column span (`/ColSpan 2`, passes), but no row span (`/RowSpan`, `it.fails`), because `\DErowspan` is a `\vbox` and the class does not tag the cell. Fixing that needs a tagpdf cell attribute in `class/docedit.cls` (owner or class work). Round 11 (lane V-EXP-033-048): done through tagpdf's `table/multirow` key from the latex-lab table test phase (`\DErowspan{rows}{content}`, preamble `testphase={table}`); criterion 4 is ticked. The owner should accept the test-phase module (ADR 0319, V41). Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criterion 3 stays unticked and nothing new could be ticked. The borders of a span are drawn once (round 8 fix). The shading of a row span colours the first row only: colortbl paints each later row's cell after the first row's hanging content, so colouring the covered rows would hide that content, and drawing one rectangle over the span needs the covered rows' heights, which the converter does not measure and no later pass of the class provides. This is a missing measure, not an open owner decision (ADR 0143, question 2).


## Typed emitters

### EXP-042 Emit text and generated identifiers safely

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1a | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §4, §3.2 (Spaces); review S-06, M-7 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want the `text` and `generatedId` emitters from the first demo on, so that body text never carries TeX into the engine.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a string, when `text` writes it, then `\ { } $ & # _ % ^ ~` are escaped.
- [x] Given a no-break space or a soft hyphen, when `text` writes it, then it is mapped to the matching TeX form; given control characters and bidirectional override characters, then they are removed; and the output is NFC-normalised.
- [x] Given a `[` that follows a line break or an item, when `text` writes it, then it is guarded so it cannot be read as an optional argument.
- [x] Given a label, macro name or file name, when `generatedId` produces it, then it derives from a counter or a hash and never from user input, and two different inputs never give the same name in one bundle.
- [x] Given a string made of every special character, when it is exported and the PDF's text is extracted, then the extracted text equals the input.

**Evidence:** `packages/export-latex/src/emit.test.ts` (describe `text emitter`: escapes every special character, guards brackets and maps no-break space and soft hyphen, removes control and bidirectional characters with NFC, and a fast-check property that the TeX decodes back to the cleaned input). The guard `[` and `]` are always braced, which satisfies the criterion without a line-break check. Round 2 (2026-11-13), criterion 4: `packages/export-latex/src/emit.bundle.test.ts` (150 distinct anchors give 150 distinct `\DEanchor` numbers, and 150 distinct paragraph styles give 150 distinct `\DES` macro names, in one converted bundle) and `emit.strings.test.ts` (`generatedId` is injective over counters). Criterion 5 (2026-11-13, packet X1): `packages/export-latex/src/typeset.compile.test.ts` (describe `EXP-042 criterion 5`) compiles three strings that hold every special character in the Podman sandbox and checks that `pdftotext` returns each one unchanged.

**Notes:** Batch 2026-11-13 (packet X1). Criterion 4 was unticked in round 1 (the bundle part was not tested) and is ticked in round 2, see Evidence. The emitter part is tested in `packages/export-latex/src/emit.strings.test.ts` (`generatedId` is injective over counters, rejects negative and non-integer input, and was made to reject negatives in this batch), but "two different inputs never give the same name in one bundle" is a bundle-level property of the converter, not yet tested. Criterion 5 is ticked in packet X1 (2026-11-13) after a compile in the sandbox, see Evidence; the compile skips where the sandbox cannot run. The other emitters are EXP-043 and EXP-044. The `text` rule on leading, trailing and repeated spaces is EXP-025.

### EXP-043 Emit PDF strings, key-value options and URLs safely

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §4; 03 §8 (I9); review S-06 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** EXP-042

As a maintainer, I want the `pdfString`, `kvValue` and `url` emitters, so that alternative text, metadata, options and link targets cannot inject TeX.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a bookmark, a metadata field or an alternative text, when `pdfString` writes it, then it uses PDF string encoding and no TeX command in the input survives as a command.
- [x] Given a value for any key-value option, when `kvValue` writes it, then it is braced and `,`, `=`, `]` and braces in the value are neutralised, so the value cannot add, end or change an option.
- [ ] Given a link target, when `url` writes it, then only the schemes of invariant I9 pass (so `run:` and `file:` do not), and characters that are special in TeX arguments are percent-encoded.
- [ ] Given each of the three emitters, when the converter's output is searched, then every place that writes such a value goes through the emitter.

**Evidence:** `packages/export-latex/src/emit.strings.test.ts` (describe `kvValue (EXP-043 criterion 2)`: for any string the output is braced and, once the wrapper, the `\DEstr` command and hex digits are removed, nothing remains, so `,`, `=`, `]` and braces cannot reach the option list; the payload decodes back to the cleaned value) and `packages/export-latex/src/emit.test.ts` (describe `pdfString and kvValue emitters`, hex-only property).

**Notes:** Batch 2026-11-13 (packet X1). Criterion 1 is unticked: `pdfString` writes UTF-8 hexadecimal digits that `docedit.lua` decodes into catcode-12 characters, not a PDF text string. The UTF-16BE form with a byte order mark (`pdfHexString`) is tested in `emit.strings.test.ts`, but which form bookmarks and metadata use is for the class and needs the compile check. Criterion 3 is unticked: the `url` emitter refuses every scheme outside I9 (tested), and hex-encodes the whole target so `#`, `%`, `&`, `$`, `~` and `_` never reach TeX. The literal rule to percent-encode TeX-special characters is not implemented, because percent-encoding `%` would change the meaning of existing escapes; this is recorded as an open question in `docs/adr/0115-export-latex-backlog-batch.md`. Criterion 4 is unticked: "every place" needs the EXP-045 guard check, which is not yet written. Round 3 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): criterion 4 stays unticked. `packages/export-latex/src/emit.output.search.test.ts` puts a probe with `\write18`, braces, `%` and `^^` into the alternative text, the figure placeholder, a heading, a link target and title, and the document title. No raw probe reaches a `.tex` file, and each place carries the probe in its emitted form. Two gaps stay open: the link title is written by `pdfHexString` (`inline.ts`, UTF-16BE), not `pdfString`, although the attribute table (`attributes.ts`, `mark:link.title`) names `pdfString`; and the probe reaches only the places one document can reach (`hidden_region.title`, `source_def` fields and bibliography text are not probed). Which emitter the link title uses is an open question for the owner (EXP-030 owns the link dictionary). Round 9 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13), criterion 1 stays unticked: `packages/export-latex/src/pdfstring.compile.test.ts` compiles a figure without a picture whose alternative text holds `\textbf`, `\input`, `%` and `^^5c`, and `pdftotext` returns the placeholder `[figure: ...]` unchanged (passes). The same file checks the document title: the compiled PDF has no `Title` field at all, not even for a plain ASCII title, so `\hypersetup{pdftitle=...}` (`convert.ts`) does not reach the metadata. That test is marked `it.fails` until the title is written; the cause is not found (the value is `{\DEstr{...}}`, decoded by `docedit.str`, and hyperref writes nothing). Bookmarks are not checked, because no outline reader is available to the test, and the "PDF string encoding" wording is not met by the hex-to-catcode path. Owner or class work needed for the title. Round 10 (lane V-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-13): the title is not lost. The compiled PDF carries it in XMP `dc:title` (probe in the sandbox: `/Title` is absent from the Info dictionary under `\DocumentMetadata` with PDF 2.0 and PDF/UA, and the XMP holds the plain title). `packages/export-latex/src/pdfstring.compile.test.ts` now reads XMP and passes as an ordinary test (no `it.fails`), with the special-character title from round 9. Lane U-EXP-033-048 (2026-11-14): bookmarks are now checked by `packages/export-latex/src/exp-043-bookmarks.compile.test.ts` (Podman sandbox, preview profile, outline read with `test/exp-023-pdf-links.ts`): a heading whose text has `%`, parentheses, `&`, `#`, `~`, `$`, `]`, `^^5c`, braces and `_` comes out in the outline as typed. A heading with a backslash command (`\textbf{BOLD}`) loses the backslash and the letter after it, a product defect in the heading bookmark path; the test records it with `it.fails` until it is fixed. Round 8 (lane U-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-14): the cause of the backslash defect is likely the bookmark string. hyperref writes the outline title as a PDF literal string without escaping its backslashes, so `\t` in `\textbf` reads as a tab. Doubling each backslash in `\DE@strexp` (`class/docedit.lua`, `docedit.strpdf`) fixed the outline, but the same string reaches the XMP `dc:title` as raw text and showed `\\`, which broke `pdfstring.compile.test.ts`; the change was reverted, the defect stays open (`it.fails` in `exp-043-bookmarks.compile.test.ts`). The fix needs a separate string path for the bookmark, which is not chosen here (open question for the class owner). Criterion 1 stays unticked: that defect, the "PDF string encoding" wording (`pdfString` writes hex digits for `\DEstr`, not a PDF text string, as noted above). Lane w2 (2026-11-15): nothing new could be ticked. Criterion 4: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-043-emitter-places.test.ts` (15 tests, Node.js, converter output only) takes the places from `ATTRIBUTE_EMITTERS` (`attributes.ts`): each of the twelve attributes that name `pdfString`, `kvValue` or `url` has a probe, and a table entry without a probe fails. Eleven places carry the probe only in the form of their emitter (`\DEstr{hex}` or a percent-encoded `\DEhref{hex}` target), which closes the second gap of round 3 (`hidden_region.title`, the `source_def` link fields, the bibliography). The alternative text of a figure and of an inline image is also checked with its picture present, as the braced `kvValue` argument of `\DEimg`. One gap is left and keeps criterion 4 unticked: `mark:link.title` is written by `pdfHexString`, not by the `pdfString` the table names; the test lists it as a known other route and fails when that changes. Whether the table should name `pdfHexString` as a ninth emitter, or the link title should go through `pdfString`, is the owner's choice (EXP-030 owns the link dictionary); the code is unchanged. Criteria 1 and 3 wait for the same owner answers as before: the bookmark string path and the "PDF string encoding" wording (criterion 1), and whether hex encoding of the whole target stands for the percent-encoding rule (criterion 3, `docs/adr/0115-export-latex-backlog-batch.md`).

### EXP-044 Emit lengths, colours and enumerations safely

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §4; 01 §8 (Lengths); review S-06 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** EXP-042

As a maintainer, I want the `dimen`, `color` and `enumLookup` emitters, so that lengths, colours and names that select behaviour cannot carry TeX.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a length in twentieths of a point, when `dimen` writes it, then it divides by 20 and writes `bp`, and a value outside the safe range is clamped to that range.
- [x] Given a colour, when `color` writes it, then exactly six hexadecimal digits are written; anything else is not written and a diagnostic names the block.
- [x] Given a language, alignment, format or bullet character, when `enumLookup` writes it, then the value selects an entry in a fixed table and the value itself is never written.
- [x] Given a value that is not in the table, when `enumLookup` runs, then the table's documented default is used and a diagnostic names the block.

**Evidence:** `packages/export-latex/src/emit.test.ts` (describe `dimen, color, enumLookup, identifiers`: `dimen(230)` is `11.5bp`, a value beyond the range is clamped; `enumLookup` writes only the table's own entries, and `known` is false for a value outside the table, with the fallback returned). The tables used by `blocks.ts`, `page.ts` and `props.ts` go through the same function. Round 2 (2026-11-13), criteria 2 and 4: the callers now go through `colourOf` and `enumOf` in `packages/export-latex/src/context.ts`, which raise `colour_invalid` and `enum_unknown` with the block id. Tests: `packages/export-latex/src/diagnostics.test.ts` (a run colour and a table cell colour that are not six hex digits give `colour_invalid` for their block, and no value but six hex digits is written; an unknown paragraph alignment or highlight gives `enum_unknown` for its paragraph, and the fallback is written; a property over unknown alignments). The other call sites (label alignment, bullet, figure, page number format, section start, tab lists, export options) use the same helper and are not tested one by one.

**Notes:** Batch 2026-11-13 (packet X1). Criteria 2 and 4 were unticked in round 1: the diagnostic that names the block was raised by the converter's callers, not by `color` or `enumLookup`, and no test of the converter's diagnostics existed yet. Round 2 (2026-11-13) decision, to be confirmed by the owner (ADR 0115, items 8 and 9): a colour or lookup that belongs to no block (a style definition, a tab list, a page setup's page number format, an export option) raises its diagnostic with a null block id, because the spec names no other owner for it. Criterion 4 covers every user-value lookup except the converter's constant `'a-4f'`. `color` returning null for any other value is tested in `emit.test.ts`.

### EXP-045 Allow TeX output only through the emitters

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §4; 03 §8 (I11); ADR 0007; review S-06 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** EXP-042, EXP-043, EXP-044

As a maintainer, I want it to be impossible to concatenate a user-controlled value into TeX without an emitter, so that a new feature cannot reopen the injection holes closed by the review.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the converter's source, when the CI check runs, then any code path that writes a user-controlled value to the bundle without going through one of the eight emitters (`text`, `pdfString`, `kvValue`, `url`, `dimen`, `color`, `enumLookup`, `generatedId`) fails the build.
- [x] Given the CDM schema, when a test lists every attribute whose value can reach output, then each has an entry that names its emitter, and an attribute without an entry fails the test.
- [ ] Given the raw writer, when its callers are listed, then only the emitters call it.
- [ ] Given a new context that user strings can reach, when it is added, then it is rejected in review until an emitter covers it.

**Evidence:** `packages/export-latex/src/attributes.test.ts` (describe `EXP-045 attribute table`): every node, projection node and mark attribute of `CDM1` has an entry in `ATTRIBUTE_EMITTERS`, the table names no other attribute, and every entry is one of the eight emitters or null (criterion 2). `packages/export-latex/src/emit.guard.test.ts` searches the package for a cast to `Tex` or a use of the constructor outside `emit.ts`; it is a search, not a build check (criteria 1 and 3, part).

**Notes:** Invariant I11 (grammars on every attribute) is the other half and belongs to CDM. Round 2 (export-latex, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays unticked, because `gatedMath` writes math that passed the gate (09 §6.3) without one of the eight emitters, and the criterion lists the eight only (ADR 0115, item 10, for the owner). Criterion 3 stays unticked: `line` takes only `Tex`, but no list of its callers is written as a test. Round 3 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): `packages/export-latex/src/emit.raw-writer.test.ts` pins the modules that call `line` (`blocks.ts`, `convert.ts`, `flow.ts`, `page.ts`, `tables.ts`), so a new caller fails the test until a reviewer lists it. Those callers are the converter's structural modules, not the emitters, so "only the emitters call it" is still not met and criterion 3 stays unticked. Criterion 4 is a review, not a test. The attribute table's `null` entries mean that the value never reaches the bundle. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): nothing new could be ticked. Criterion 1 waits on the owner's answer to ADR 0115, item 10 (is `gatedMath` a ninth emitter?), which docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md does not give; the build already refuses a plain `string` where `Tex` is required (`emit.guard.test.ts`), and `fontFile` and `gatedMath` are the two checked constructors outside the eight. Criterion 3 as worded ("only the emitters call it") is not what the code does (the structural modules call `line`), so it needs the owner to accept the pinned list or to reword it. Criterion 4 is a review rule, not a test.

### EXP-046 Fuzz the emitters per context and per language

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | L | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §4, §14; 10 §3.4, §5 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** EXP-045, EXP-114, SEC-001

As a maintainer, I want adversarial tests of every emitter in every context, so that an escaping mistake is found by a machine before it is found by an attacker.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given random and mutated strings for the `text` emitter, when each is exported and compiled, then the extracted text of the PDF equals the input.
- [ ] Given random and mutated strings for each other emitter, when each is exported and compiled, then the job compiles and a trace of the token stream contains no control sequence that the emitter did not write.
- [x] Given each context in which an emitter is used and strings in several languages and scripts (including right-to-left, CJK and combining marks), when the fuzzer runs, then every combination is covered.
- [x] Given seeds from known attacks (`\input`, `\write18`, `^^` notation, unbalanced braces, `]` and `,` in options), when the fuzzer runs, then they are included in every run.
- [ ] Given a failure, when it is found, then the input is stored as a fixture of the corpus.

**Evidence:** criterion 1 (re-ticked by the round 6 fixer, group B3): `packages/export-latex/src/emit.compile.fuzz.test.ts` compiles one document in the sandbox, one paragraph per string, and checks that each input is one line of `pdftotext -raw` output (ligature glyphs expanded by NFKC). The batch is all 23 known-attack seeds, unfiltered, 4 words that hold `fi`, `ff`, `fl`, `ffi` and `ffl`, and 120 fast-check samples (seed 46) of random strings over printable ASCII and the space (upper- and lower-case letters, digits, every TeX special, hyphen, quotes) and of seeds with a special character inserted; 146 distinct strings after removing duplicates, up to 39 characters, all found (local run, 2026-11-13, Podman sandbox). A second test in the same file fails if any seed does not fit the batch or is missing from it, or if the alphabet loses lower-case letters. Criteria 3 and 4: `packages/export-latex/src/emit.fuzz.test.ts` (fast-check, 120 runs per property; for each of eleven groups of languages and scripts (Latin, Greek, Cyrillic, Arabic and Hebrew with directional marks, CJK, Devanagari, combining marks, emoji, TeX specials, controls): `text` in three contexts (block start, after a space, explicit spaces), `pdfString` and `kvValue`, `url` with six prefixes, `color` and `enumLookup`; `dimen` and `generatedId` over integers). Each property starts with the known-attack seeds (`examples`), so the seeds are in every run.

**Notes:** Batch 2026-11-13 (packet X1, round 4). The fuzzer checks the grammar of each output, not a compile. `text` output is read back through its escapes and must equal the cleaned input, with no bare TeX special character or backslash; the hex forms decode to the cleaned input. Criteria 1 and 2 need the sandbox and a trace of the compiled token stream (`services/export-worker`, Podman), not run here. Criterion 5 needs a stored corpus of failing inputs, not built; no failure has been found, so nothing is stored (ADR 0115, item 20). Lane export-latex-5, round 7 (2026-11-13): the store and replay mechanism is built in `packages/export-latex/src/emit.corpus.test.ts` (describes `EXP-046 criterion 5`): a fixture is `{emitter, input}` in `packages/export-latex/test/emitter-corpus/`, named by a SHA-256 prefix, and every stored fixture is replayed through the grammar checks of `text`, `pdfString`, `kvValue` and `url` on each run. The folder is empty, because no failure has been found; the replay test checks the format only. Criterion 5 stays unticked until a failure is stored. The corpus package is not in this lane, so the folder location is a proposal (ADR 0143, item 3). Criteria 1 and 2 are unchanged (sandbox compile and token trace). `url` hex-encodes the whole target, so its output holds no TeX special; see EXP-043 Notes for the percent-encoding question. Round 6 (lane U-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-14), criterion 2 stays unticked: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-046-trace.compile.test.ts` (Podman sandbox, 2 of 2 passed) compiles a baseline and a random batch of `pdfString` (figure alternative text) and `url` (link target) strings with `\tracingcommands1` switched on after `\begin{document}`, and finds no unexpandable control sequence in the random compile that the baseline lacks; a `\special` injected into the body is reported (negative control). Two limits: the trace is at level 1 only, because `\tracingmacros2` with level 2 writes about 200 MB of log for this batch (hyperref purifies every link target), so expandable macros are not traced; and the `kvValue`, `dimen`, `color`, `enumLookup` and `generatedId` emitters have no batch yet. Round 9 (export-latex-5, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 is ticked on one sandbox compile of a batch (one paragraph per string), not one compile per string. The batch leaves out hyphens, quotes, lower-case `fi` and `ff` (ligatures, not what the emitter does) and the glyphs a default font lacks, so those are not tested by it. Criterion 2 still needs the token trace of the compiled job, and criterion 5 needs a failure to store. Round 6 review (group B3): criterion 1 is unticked again, because the batch holds no lower-case letter and omits the named attacks (see Evidence); it can be ticked when the alphabet includes lower-case letters (leaving out only `fi` and `ff`) and every seed is compiled. Round 6 fix (group B3): the alphabet is now printable ASCII with no exclusions. The class loads fonts with `Ligatures=TeXOff`, so `--` and quotes stay as typed; the font's `fi`/`ff` ligatures are compared through NFKC instead of being left out. Every seed is compiled, and criterion 1 is ticked again. Characters outside ASCII are not in this compiled batch (the scripts of criterion 3 are checked by grammar in `emit.fuzz.test.ts`, not compiled), because they depend on the fallback fonts. Round 8 (lane U-EXP-033-048, 2026-11-14), criterion 2, other emitters: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-046-others.trace.compile.test.ts` (Podman sandbox, machine route, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`, 2 of 2 passed) compiles a baseline and a random batch with the same alphabet and seeds for the document title (`kvValue`, in the preamble, so the trace starts after the `\documentclass` line), the paragraph alignment (`enumLookup`), the cell shading (`color`) and the paragraph indent (`dimen`, integers), and finds no extra control sequence at level 1. `generatedId` takes a counter, not a string, so no batch reaches it. Criterion 2 stays unticked, because the trace is level 1 only (the same limit as above) and the owner has not said whether level 1 meets "a trace of the token stream". Lane w2 (2026-11-17): nothing new could be ticked. Neither open point is in docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md. Criterion 2 needs the owner's word on the trace level and a batch for `generatedId`; criterion 5 needs a real failure to store, and none has been found.

## Fonts

### EXP-047 Load fonts from the manifest by hash

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 1b | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §5, §2, §8; 02 §6 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** BE-047, FE-061, FE-062
