**Evidence:** criterion 2 (lane U-EXP-081-096): `services/export-worker/test/exp-083-markup.e2e.test.ts` (a paragraph with a deletion and an insertion, exported with `suggestions` `markup` through the job resource, the worker and the Podman sandbox; the PDF's extracted text, read with `pdftotext`, holds `Keep`, `old`, `new` and `text.`). Run with `pnpm vitest run services/export-worker/test/exp-083-markup.e2e.test.ts` (Podman, worker image `localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4`, 2026-11-13). To allow the option, `services/export-worker/src/profile.ts` now accepts `markup` (it refused it as "comes with EXP-083"). criterion 5: `packages/export-latex/src/suggestions.markup.test.ts` (the job's diagnostics carry `markup_pagination`, an info, once, when the document has suggestions in markup mode; none for `accept`, `reject` or a document without suggestions). The mode and the markup are in `packages/export-latex/src/suggestions.ts` (`markupRun`, `suggestionHides`) and `packages/export-latex/src/inline.ts` (`runTex`). Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/suggestions.markup.test.ts`.

**Evidence (lane V-EXP-081-096, round 5, 2026-11-13):** criterion 1: `services/export-worker/test/exp-083-markup-colour.e2e.test.ts` compiles a document with one `del` and one `ins` run in `markup` mode in the Podman sandbox (worker image `localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4`, `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`), and reads the PDF's page content with `services/export-worker/test/pdf-colours.ts` (inflates the FlateDecode streams and reads the `rg` operators): the insertion colour `#1F5FAD` and the deletion colour `#B3261E` are both set, and `old` and `new` are in the extracted text. Run with `pnpm vitest run services/export-worker/test/exp-083-markup-colour.e2e.test.ts` (1 test, about 23 s). Criterion 4: the same test checks the TeX source: the insertion is `\DEc{1F5FAD}{\DEul{...}}` (underline) and the deletion `\DEc{B3261E}{\DEst{...}}` (strike-through, `\strikeThrough` in `docedit.cls`). The test reads the source; it does not look at the drawn underline or strike line in the PDF.

**Evidence (lane w2, 2026-11-17):** criterion 3: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-083-rows-atoms.test.ts` (6 tests: the cells of a deleted row get the strike-through and deletion colour while the other row stays unmarked; every cell of an inserted table and of a deleted table is marked; a row mark and a cell run's own mark of the same kind give one underline; an equation with an `ins` or `del` mark is wrapped like a run, and so is one in a deleted paragraph; `accept`, `reject` and the argument are unchanged) and `packages/export-latex/src/exp-083-rows-atoms.compile.test.ts` (2 tests, compiled in the Podman sandbox of the worker image: page 1 is rendered with `pdftoppm` at 100 dpi, a plain table has no blue or red pixels, a deleted row gives red pixels, an inserted table gives blue pixels, the text of every cell is still in the PDF's text, and equations with an insertion and a deletion compile and draw both colours; about 72 s on this machine, 2026-11-17). The code is the push-down of row and table marks to their paragraphs in `settleSuggestions` (`packages/export-latex/src/suggestions.ts`) and the wrapping of `image`, `equation`, `field`, `citation` and `form_field` atoms in `inlineTex` (`packages/export-latex/src/inline.ts`). Run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/export-latex/src/exp-083-rows-atoms packages/export-latex/src/suggestions`.

**Notes:** The non-colour cue is inferred from the accessibility principles of PRODUCT.md. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criterion 3 is ticked, so all five are ticked. A row or table is marked by marking each paragraph in it, not by a frame or a bar at its edge: the cue is the one text has (colour plus underline or strike-through), and an empty cell shows nothing. An atom is underlined or struck as a run is; for a picture the line is drawn over the box, which the compile test checks only for equations. A footnote reference, a break, a tab and an anchor with a mark are not wrapped. Round 5 (lane V-EXP-081-096): criteria 1 and 4 are ticked as above; criterion 4 rests on the source-level underline and strike-through, which the PDF test does not draw-check. Criteria 3 (whole rows and tables) is still open. How `fmt` suggestions and authors are shown in markup is not specified (Open questions). Criterion 2 is ticked (lane U-EXP-081-096, 2026-11-13: the worker now accepts `markup`, and the PDF text holds both kinds of text); criteria 1, 3 and 4 are unticked. The markup is in the TeX source only: an insertion is `\DEc` in the insertion colour around `\DEul`, a deletion `\DEc` in the deletion colour around `\DEst`, both through the colour emitter, and the test reads that source. The PDF's colour, underline and strike-through, and the extracted text of criterion 2, need a LuaLaTeX run, which this package does not do. Criterion 3 is partly built (lane EXP-079-091, round 2, ADR 0227 decision 1): a paragraph with an `ins` or `del` mark has its text runs marked in markup mode (`blockMarks` and `runMarks` in `packages/export-latex/src/inline.ts`; test `packages/export-latex/src/suggestions.block-markup.test.ts`). A row, a table and atoms (images, fields, equations) with a suggestion still print unmarked, so the criterion stays open. The older assertion in `suggestions.markup.test.ts` that counts one deletion colour macro now sees two (the struck paragraph is marked); that test is not this lane's and is left as it is. The colours (`#1F5FAD` for insertions, `#B3261E` for deletions) are a proposed default (docs/adr/0142-export-latex-4-backlog-batch.md), not an owner decision.

### EXP-084 Show comments in the margin

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | should | Owner |

**Spec:** 09 §9.1, §10; ADR 0015; 05 §6.2 · **Requirements:** R5, R8 · **Depends on:** EXP-005, EXP-043, SYN-032, PRO-059

As an owner, I want to see the comments of a review in the PDF margin, so that I can print or circulate the review.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given `comments` is `margin`, when exported, then the threads at the exported `seq` appear in the margin next to their target range or block; given `none`, no comments appear.
- [ ] Given comment ranges, when the converter reads them, then they come from the document service's mapped ranges at the exported `seq`, because comments are not in the document.
- [ ] Given a thread whose range is detached, when exported with `margin`, then the thread is listed with a warning instead of being anchored to the wrong place.
- [ ] Given comment text and author names, when written, then they go through the typed emitters.
- [ ] Given tagging is on, when the PDF is inspected, then the margin comments do not break the reading order of the body.

**Notes:** The default of `comments`, the display of resolved threads, and where author names come from (the service holds only surrogates; 08 §13) are not specified (Open questions). The margin package must work with tagging (EXP-015). Round 2 (lane EXP-079-091, ADR 0227 decision 4): no margin writer exists in `packages/export-latex`, and the converter does not read the `comments` option, so no criterion is ticked. Round 4 (lane V-EXP-081-096, 2026-11-13): still no criterion ticked; the gap is the comment ranges in the payload from the service, a margin macro in the house class, and a LuaLaTeX run (ADR 0322 item p). Lane w2 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing ticked, no code written. Checked again against the tree: `services/export-worker/src/profile.ts` still refuses `comments: margin` and the converter has no comments input. SYN-032 now maps thread ranges in the authority (`packages/authority/src/ranges.ts`) and the service holds threads (`services/document-service/src/control-plane/thread-routes.ts`), but the export worker reads only checkpoints and commits (`services/export-worker/src/content.ts`); it has no read of threads, and the "mapped ranges at `seq`" of criterion 2 are not stored per `seq`, so they would be mapped from the last stored anchors through the commits in between. What is still needed, in this order, and what is for the owner: (1) the owner's answer on author names (the service holds surrogates, 08 §13: a name must come from the tenant's directory, and the worker has no access to it) and on the display of resolved threads and the default of `comments`; without that the text of the margin note cannot be fixed; (2) a thread input for the converter (the shape of ADR 0228 decision 1 is the candidate: thread id, anchor block, offsets in the paragraph, `detached`, messages with author label), written by the control plane next to `sealing`; (3) a margin macro and a wider margin in the house class, which is a new class release (EXP-015 criterion 4: `CLASS_VERSION`, `releases.json`) and a check that `\marginpar` or a replacement keeps the reading order of the tagged PDF (criterion 5); (4) the worker lifting the refusal in `profile.ts`. Building (2) and (3) before (1) would fix a note layout and a payload on a guess, so nothing was started.


## Other targets

### EXP-085 Download the LaTeX bundle

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §11, §2, §9.3 item 7; 00 §4 (R5) · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-004, EXP-011, EXP-073

As an author, I want to download the LaTeX source of my export, so that I can compile it myself or keep it with the PDF.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `target` is `tex`, when the job completes, then the result is an archive with `main.tex`, `styles.tex`, `docedit.cls`, `assets/`, `map.json` and `manifest.json`, and no auxiliary files or logs.
- [x] Given the archive, when it is read, then it lists the fonts it needs by hash and licence, because fonts are not part of the bundle, and it states how they are obtained.
- [x] Given the archive, when it is opened, then it carries a notice that it should be compiled with the same restrictions as the service uses: no shell escape, no network, paranoid file access and LuaTeX 1.17 or later.
- [x] Given the archive, when it is opened, then it includes the licence of the house class and the licence manifest of the packages it relies on.
- [x] Given the archive, when it is compiled with the documented command and the listed fonts, then the PDF's text equals that of the service's PDF.

**Evidence:** criteria 1 to 3: `packages/export-latex/src/archive.test.ts` (reads the ustar members back: `main.tex`, `styles.tex`, `docedit.cls`, `map.json`, `manifest.json` and the `assets/` picture are present, and no `.aux`, `.log`, `.out`, `.toc` or `.pdf` member is; the README lists each font file by SHA-256, family, role and licence, says how to place the fonts and check their hashes, and gives the compile restrictions). Run on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/archive.test.ts`.

**Evidence (lane V-EXP-081-096, round 5, 2026-11-13):** criterion 1: `services/export-worker/src/exp-085-v-tex.job.test.ts` (a `tex` job through the JobRunner and MemoryJobQueue, real export handler, no compile: `completed`, one stored archive `source.tar` as `application/x-tar`, with `main.tex`, `styles.tex`, `docedit.cls`, `map.json`, `manifest.json` and `README.txt` and no `.aux`, `.log`, `.out`, `.toc` or `.pdf` member). Run with `pnpm vitest run services/export-worker/src/exp-085-v-tex.job.test.ts` (2 tests; corrected from 3 by the reviewer, 2026-11-13). Criterion 5: `services/export-worker/test/exp-085-archive.e2e.test.ts` run with `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1` (Podman, worker image `localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4`, sandbox ready, `pdftotext`): the sandbox compile of the bundle and the archive compiled with the README command both give the same squashed PDF text; 1 test passed in about 60 s. The earlier skip is no longer hit on this machine.

**Evidence (lane w2, 2026-11-15):** criterion 4: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-085-licences.test.ts` (5 tests, Node.js, `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/export-latex/src/exp-085-licences.test.ts`). The archive is read back: `LICENCE.txt` equals the project's `LICENSE` file (Apache-2.0) word for word; `licences.json` names the class, its version and licence and lists the TeX Live packages with their licences; every package or class that `class/docedit.cls` loads with `\RequirePackage` or `\LoadClass` has a row; the README says where both are. The rows are in `packages/export-latex/src/licences.ts`; they were read on 2026-11-15 from `tlpkg/texlive.tlpdb` of the worker image `localhost/docedit-export-worker:s4` (image id 1b32f4ae8043) with Podman on this Windows machine. `services/export-worker/test/exp-085-archive.e2e.test.ts` (criterion 5) was run again with the two new members in the archive, with `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1` and `DOCEDIT_CONTAINER_CGROUPS=disabled`: 1 test passed.

**Notes:** Lane w2 (2026-11-15): criterion 4 is ticked. The manifest in the archive lists the packages the class loads by name and the ones tagging and font loading bring in (`tagpdf`, `latex-lab`, `luaotfload`, `l3kernel`, `l3packages`), 18 rows; the packages those load in turn are only in the image's full manifest (EXP-073), and the README says so. This reading of 'the packages it relies on' is the lane's; the spec does not say how deep the list goes. The rows are a checked-in copy of the image's database, not read at job time: a new image release needs them read again (the test catches a new `\RequirePackage`, not a changed revision).

**Notes:** Criterion 1 was unticked by the evidence audit (2026-11-13): `texArchive` is tested as a function only, and `services/export-worker/src/job.ts` refuses every target but `pdf` and `cdm` (`this worker exports pdf and cdm, not tex`), so no job returns the archive. 09 §11 says the archive "compiles outside the service" but 09 §2 keeps fonts out of the bundle (Open questions). Criterion 4 is unticked in part: the README names the class licence (Apache-2.0, tested), but the archive does not include the licence manifest of the packages, which the README places in the export worker image (EXP-073, `services/export-worker`); the archive does not carry it. Criterion 5 is unticked: it needs a LuaLaTeX compile of the archive, which this package does not run and no LuaLaTeX run was made. Round 3 (lane U-EXP-081-096, 2026-11-13): `services/export-worker/test/exp-085-archive.e2e.test.ts` compiles the archive in the worker image with the README's command (run twice), and compares its PDF text with the sandbox compile of the same bundle. It has not passed yet: the sandbox readiness probe failed on the loaded Podman machine (`podman info` and `image exists` took 43 s and 54 s), so the test skips, and criterion 5 stays unticked. The test is a skip, not a pass. Round 4 (lane V-EXP-081-096, 2026-11-13): the test was run twice, once plain and once with `DOCEDIT_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`; both runs skipped at `sandboxReady()`, which takes about 160 s on the loaded Podman machine and reports no ready sandbox. The skip reason is not printed, because the `describe.skipIf` block skips before the reason is shown, so the cause is not identified. Criterion 5 stays unticked, and the next step is to read the probe's reason on a quiet machine. Two timeouts in `services/export-worker/src/container.ts` and `sandbox-ready.ts` were raised for this (30 s to 120 s). Criterion 4 (licence manifest in the archive) is not built: `archive.ts` takes no licence manifest, and the manifest is made by `scripts/licence-manifest.ts`. Round 5 (lane V-EXP-081-096): criteria 1 and 5 are ticked (Evidence above). `scripts/licence-manifest.ts` is not in the repository, so the worker has no licence manifest to add to the archive; criterion 4 stays unticked and needs EXP-073 (or the owner) to supply the manifest file.

### EXP-086 Export the canonical JSON

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 1b | S | should | Integrating developer |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 03 §2; 05 §6.2 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-004, CDM-010

As an integrating developer, I want the document's canonical JSON as an export target, so that I can keep or process the content without the editor.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `target` is `cdm`, when the job completes, then the result is the envelope (`cdm`, `requires`, `doc`) at the requested `seq` in the RFC 8785 form with the CDM's canonical-form rules.
- [x] Given the output, when its SHA-256 hash is compared with the hash the conformance kit computes for the same document, then they are equal.
- [x] Given the output, when it is read, then it holds no comments, grants or history, which are not part of the document.
- [x] Given the job, when it runs, then it follows the same states, events, permission and `no-export` rules as other targets.

**Evidence:** criterion 4 (lane U-EXP-081-096, round 2, 2026-11-13): `services/export-worker/src/exp-086-cdm.job.test.ts` (run through the control plane's JobRunner and MemoryJobQueue with the real export handler; a `cdm` job ends `completed`, its stage reports are `converting 5` then `converting 50` with no `compiling` stage, no compile runs, and only `output.json` is stored, as `application/json`, equal to `cdmWriter`'s text and SHA-256; a restricted view is projected as for pdf (EXP-123) and writes no hidden text; an unknown target is refused with `validation_failed`). The permission and `no-export` gates are tested at the HTTP level in `services/document-service/test/control-plane/exp-086-cdm-target.test.ts` (memory store and PostgreSQL: a viewer may request `cdm`; a caller with no role is refused; a caller restricted with `no-export` gets the same status and code for `cdm` as for `pdf`). Run with `pnpm vitest run services/export-worker/src/exp-086-cdm.job.test.ts` (4 tests) and `pnpm vitest run services/document-service/test/control-plane/exp-086-cdm-target.test.ts` (PostgreSQL in Podman). The service's test, of an earlier round: `services/document-service/test/control-plane/exp-086-cdm-target.test.ts` (memory store and PostgreSQL, 6 tests, PostgreSQL in Podman, 2026-11-13); it uses a stand-in handler, so it covers the service's rules and not the writer. Criteria 1 to 3: `packages/export-latex/src/cdm-writer.test.ts`. For each envelope case of the canonical-form fixtures (`packages/model/fixtures/canonical/index.json`, read only), `cdmWriter` gives the fixture's expected text and its SHA-256, which the independent Python kit (`packages/model/conformance/canonical.py`) computes; an envelope with `comments`, `grants` and `history` members gives output with only `cdm`, `requires` and `doc`, and the same hash. Run on 2026-11-13 with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/cdm-writer.test.ts`.

**Notes:** Phase inferred: the roadmap does not place `cdm` or `tex`; both come from the same job at little cost and R5 lists the bundle. Whether `pending_suggestions` applies to this target is open (EXP-082). Criterion 4 ticked in round 2 (lane U-EXP-081-096, 2026-11-13). The worker now has a `cdm` branch (`services/export-worker/src/job.ts`, `CDM_OUTPUT_FILE`), which runs the same content, class-version and restricted-view gates as `pdf`. "Events" is read as the job's states and its progress reports: the job runner has no separate event stream, so none is asserted (judgement for the owner to confirm). The permission check is at the HTTP level, in the document-service test.

### EXP-087 Export HTML

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 13 §12; 03 §10 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-004, CDM-005

As an author, I want HTML export, so that I can use the content in other tools.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `target` is `html`, when the job completes, then headings, paragraphs, lists with their literal labels, tables with spans, images with alternative text, links, footnotes and equations are present in a structure that matches the document.
- [x] Given text and attribute values, when written, then they are escaped for HTML, the output contains no script or event handler, and link targets use only the schemes of invariant I9.
- [x] Given paragraph and run formatting, when exported, then the resolved formatting follows the cascade of 03 §6.
- [x] Given a construct HTML cannot express (page setups, headers and footers, tab stops, form field controls), when exported, then the job's warnings name it.
- [x] Given the output, when its visible text is compared with the document's text, then they are equal.
- [ ] Given images, when the job completes, then they are delivered together with the HTML in the target's documented packaging.

**Evidence:** criteria 2 and 4 (writer level; the job's warnings are the service's part of criterion 4): `packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.test.ts`. Criterion 2: `escapeHtml` on text and attribute values, a fast-check property (seed 20261113, 200 runs) that a printable paragraph is written as its escaped text with no `<script`, a test that no script element or event handler attribute is written, and a `javascript:` link written as its text with `link_scheme_dropped`, an `https` link and an in-document target kept. Criterion 4: tab stops, page breaks, form fields and page setups are named in the diagnostics, and headers and footers (stories) as `headers_footers_omitted`. Criterion 5 (round 3, lane EXP-079-091): `packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.visible-text.test.ts` (one test: a heading, nested bullet and numbered lists with their labels, a table with a column span and a closing paragraph; the visible text read back from the output, with the list labels counted as visible text, equals the hand-written text of the document). The paragraph test in `html-writer.test.ts` covers paragraphs. Footnotes, pictures and equations are not in that comparison. Criterion 3 (lane U-EXP-081-096, round 4): `packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-cascade.html.test.ts` (bold from the paragraph style; a direct `value: false` mark overriding it; bold and italic from a paragraph style and a character style; a colour from a style, with small capitals from a style named `caps_plain`). Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-cascade.html.test.ts packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.test.ts` (19 tests, passing on 2026-11-13). Reviewer, U4 group 1 round 4: criterion 3 unticked. The cascade is real for bold, italic, strike, underline, vertical alignment, colour, language and hidden (the five tests pass), but `fontFamily`, `fontSize` and `highlight` of the resolved run are neither written (HTML can: a `style` on the span, as colour is) nor named in a diagnostic, so formatting is lost without a warning. Tick again when they are written, or when each is named by a warning and the spec says that is enough. Fixed, U4 group 1 round 4 (fixer), criterion 3 ticked again at writer level: `html-writer.ts` now writes the whole resolved run, so `fontFamily` and `fontSize` are written as `font-family` and `font-size` (twips / 20, in points) on the run's `span` where they differ from the default paragraph style, whose family and size are written on `body` in the stylesheet, and `highlight` as `background-color` (the RGB values Word gives the 16 named highlights of 03 §4.5); a family outside the model's pattern or a highlight outside the list is named (`font_family_not_written`, `highlight_not_written`). Formatting marks are no longer read one by one: the overwritten mark loop for bold, italic, strike, underline and vertical alignment is gone, and `charStyle`, `fontFamily`, `fontSize`, `highlight`, `smallCaps` and `allCaps` marks no longer raise a spurious `<type>_plain` warning. Paragraph alignment and the `paragraph_layout_omitted` warning now read the resolved paragraph too, so a style's alignment is written and a style's spacing is named. `packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-fonts.html.test.ts` (11 tests: family and size of the default style on `body`; the base 11 pt; size from a paragraph style; family from a character style over a paragraph style size; a direct size mark over the style; a direct size equal to the default writes nothing; highlight from a character style and from a direct mark; family, size, colour and highlight in one `style`; alignment from a paragraph style and a direct one over it; spacing from a style named; an unsafe family and an unknown highlight named and not written). Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-fonts.html.test.ts packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-cascade.html.test.ts packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.test.ts packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.visible-text.test.ts` (4 files, 31 tests, passing on 2026-11-13, Node.js) and `tsc --noEmit -p packages/export-latex` (clean). The export job does not offer `html` yet (Notes), so "when exported" is proved at the writer, not through a job. Not ticked: criterion 1, because equations are written as LaTeX source in a `pre` element and the representation is open (Notes); criterion 6 (below). Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.test.ts` (14 tests, passing on 2026-11-13) and `tsc --noEmit -p packages/export-latex`.

**Evidence (lane w2, 2026-11-17):** criterion 4, through a job: `services/export-worker/src/exp-087-html.job.test.ts` (4 tests, JobRunner and MemoryJobQueue as in the `cdm` job test, no Podman: an `html` job completes with the states of `cdm`, stores only `output.html` as `text/html; charset=utf-8` and never calls the compiler; a document with tab stops gives `paragraph_layout_omitted` and `page_setup_omitted` as warnings in the job's result diagnostics; a restricted view is projected as for `pdf` (EXP-123); unresolved suggestions without a mode fail the job and with `accept` it completes). The worker now accepts the target (`services/export-worker/src/job.ts`, `HTML_OUTPUT_FILE`), and `page_setup_omitted` is a warning (it was `info`). Criterion 1: `packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-structure.html.test.ts` (3 tests: one document with a heading, a paragraph with a link, an inline equation and a footnote reference, a bullet list and a numbered list with their literal labels, a table with a column span, a figure with alternative text and caption, a display equation and the footnote list; each is found in its element and in document order; equations are named as source). Run with `pnpm exec vitest run --project node packages/export-latex/src/exp-087-structure services/export-worker/src/exp-087-html` (7 tests, 2026-11-17). Decisions proposed in docs/adr/0371-w2-exp-087-html-job.md.

**Notes:** Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 and 4 are ticked on the proposed representation of ADR 0371 (equations as LaTeX source, which the earlier round named as the open point; the job's output file). Criterion 6 stays unticked: the worker reads no picture bytes (`assets: {}` in `job.ts`, for every target), so a picture can only be written as its alternative text with `image_asset_missing`, and there is nothing to package. The packaging is proposed in the ADR (one file, or a tar with `index.html` and `assets/`) and needs the owner's word on tar against zip. The document service must also list `html` among its `exportTargets` for a request to be accepted; that is configuration and was not changed. Earlier: Criterion 4 unticked by the evidence audit (2026-11-13): it says the job's warnings name the construct, but the export worker refuses `html` (`job.ts` accepts only `pdf` and `cdm`), so no job carries the writer's diagnostics, and `page_setup_omitted` is written at `info`, not as a warning; the tests cover the writer's diagnostics only. Direct writer (09 §11), in `packages/export-latex/src/html-writer.ts`, exported from the package index, not added to `writer.ts` (the job's target registry is the service's). Round 1, lane EXP-079-091. Criterion 3 (unticked by the reviewer in round 4, ticked again by the round 4 fixer): each paragraph and run is resolved through the model's `resolveParagraph` and `resolveRun` (03 §6), so style formatting is written and not only direct marks. Font family, size and highlight are written as CSS on the run (see Evidence); small and all capitals are named as `caps_plain`, and paragraph spacing, indents, borders, shading and tab stops as `paragraph_layout_omitted`. Writing the default style's family and size on `body` rather than on every run, and the highlight RGB values, are representation choices the spec leaves open (proposed here, for the owner). A language is written on a run only when it differs from the document default, which is the `lang` of the html element. Criterion 6 is the job's packaging of `assets/`; the writer returns the picture list. Representation choices (open in the spec): equations as LaTeX source, not rendered; lists as `ul` and `ol` with the literal label in a `span`, styled `list-style: none`; alignment as a class with a fixed stylesheet; `markup` suggestions as `ins` and `del`, the missing option as `pending_suggestions` (error) with the text written as rejected. The paste side (HTML in and out of the clipboard) is FE's.

### EXP-088 Export Markdown

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | should | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 13 §12 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-004

As an author, I want Markdown export, so that I can move text into tools that use it.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given `target` is `md`, when the job completes, then headings, emphasis, strike, lists, tables, links, images and footnotes are written in Markdown.
- [ ] Given text that contains Markdown-special characters, when written, then they are escaped so the rendered text equals the typed text.
- [x] Given a construct Markdown cannot express (colours, underline, merged cells, page setups), when exported, then it is approximated and the job's warnings name it.
- [x] Given link targets, when written, then only the schemes of invariant I9 are used.

**Evidence:** criteria 1, 3 and 4 (writer level; the job's part of criteria 1 and 3, carrying the writer's diagnostics into the job's warnings, is the service's): `packages/export-latex/src/markdown-writer.test.ts`. Criterion 1: headings by outline level, emphasis, strike, bullet and literal numbered labels with nesting, a pipe table, links, a picture with its alternative text and asset, and a footnote and its definition. Criterion 3 (unticked by the reviewer in group 3 round 1, fixed and re-ticked in the fix round): underline, page breaks, merged cells, equations, paragraph alignment and colour are named in the diagnostics; a column span (`colspan: 2`) is written as the cell and an empty cell with one `merged_cells_flattened` warning, as a row span is; a `page_setup` in the definitions raises the warning `page_setup_omitted` and is not raised without one; colour, underline, merged cells and page setups are all warnings, not errors. Criterion 4: a `javascript:` link is written as its text with `link_scheme_dropped`, an in-document target and an `https` or `mailto` link are written, and a destination's blanks and parentheses are percent-encoded. Criterion 2 (escapes, `unescapeMd` round trip under fast-check, seed 20261113, 200 runs) is open: it checks that the escapes read back, not the rendered text, because the workspace carries no Markdown renderer. Run with `pnpm vitest run packages/export-latex/src/markdown-writer.test.ts` (23 tests) and `tsc --noEmit -p packages/export-latex`, both passing on 2026-11-13. Criteria 1 and 3 through the job (lane w2, 2026-11-17): `services/export-worker/src/exp-088-md.job.test.ts` (6 tests; an `md` job runs through the control plane's JobRunner and MemoryJobQueue with the real export handler, ends `completed` with the states of an `html` job, stores only `output.md` as `text/markdown; charset=utf-8`, never compiles, writes headings, escapes `_` and `*` in the typed text, names underline and `page_setup_omitted` as warnings in the result diagnostics, projects a restricted view without the hidden region (EXP-123), and refuses unresolved suggestions without a mode as `unsupported_feature`). Code: `services/export-worker/src/job.ts` (the `md` branch, `MD_OUTPUT_FILE`).

**Notes:** Criteria 1 and 3 unticked by the evidence audit (2026-11-13): both are stated for the job ("when the job completes", "the job's warnings name it"), but the export worker refuses `md` (`job.ts` accepts only `pdf` and `cdm`); the tests call `markdownText` directly, and the story's own Evidence calls the job part the service's. Ticked again on 2026-11-17 (lane w2): the worker now runs `md` jobs (`job.ts`), as it runs `cdm` and `html`. Direct writer (09 §11), in `packages/export-latex/src/markdown-writer.ts`, exported from the package index, not added to `writer.ts` (the job's target registry is the service's). Round 1, lane EXP-079-091. Choices the spec leaves open, all approximations: bullets are written as `-`; numbered labels are written literally and not indented for nesting (an indented ordered list would become code after a blank line); a `markup` suggestion is written with `<ins>` and `<del>`, and a block suggestion has no mark (info diagnostic); a missing `suggestions` option with suggestions present is the error `pending_suggestions` and the text is written as rejected, as the LaTeX writer does (EXP-082); display equations are written as LaTeX source in a code block, not rendered; pictures are written under `assets/`, and the job packages them (criterion 6 of 09, not covered). Criterion 2 stays open for the reason above. Lane w2, 2026-11-17: criterion 2 stays unticked; the lockfile has no Markdown renderer (markdown-it, marked, micromark or commonmark), and the criterion asks that the rendered text equals the typed text, which the `unescapeMd` round trip does not show. It needs a dependency: a CommonMark renderer in the dev dependencies of `packages/export-latex`. Round 4 (lane U-EXP-081-096): still open; the workspace has no Markdown renderer, and adding one is a root `package.json` change for the main session (needsFromOthers). EPUB, through a Pandoc JSON writer, is "later" in 09 and unscheduled in the roadmap; no story is written for it.

### EXP-089 Mark exports with the classification label

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 4 | S | could | Tenant administrator |

**Spec:** 13 §10, §12 · **Requirements:** R5, R15 · **Depends on:** EXP-004, EXP-005, UX-085

As a tenant administrator, I want the document's classification label printed on exports, so that a copy that leaves the editor still shows how it must be handled.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a document with a classification label, when a PDF is exported, then the label appears as a marking in the documented place on every page, written through the `text` emitter or an `enumLookup` of the tenant's list.
- [ ] Given a document without a label, when a PDF is exported, then no marking appears.
- [ ] Given a label change, when the document is exported again, then the bundle hash differs and a new result is produced.
- [ ] Given the other targets, when exported, then they carry the label in the way their documentation states.

**Notes:** Phase 4 follows 13 §12. Where the marking is placed is not specified (Open questions). Round 3 (lane EXP-079-091, ADR 0227 decision 9): the converter has no input for the classification label, so no criterion is ticked. Round 4 (lane U-EXP-081-096): still unticked. The placement of the marking is open in the spec and no spec default is written, so the lane did not choose one.

## DOCX export

### EXP-090 Write DOCX text, styles and numbering

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 03 §10; ADR 0004; 11 §7 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-115, CDM-005

As an author, I want a Word file of my document, so that I can send it to someone who works in Word.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given `target` is `docx`, when the job completes, then `export-docx` has written the package directly from the CDM, and it opens in Word and LibreOffice without a repair prompt (target).
- [ ] Given named styles, `basedOn` chains, direct formatting, fonts and colours, when exported, then they appear in the file as styles and direct properties, and the formatting Word resolves for the corpus equals the cascade of 03 §6.
- [x] Given multi-level numbering, when exported, then the labels shown in the file equal those of the shared algorithm.
- [x] Given any string, when written, then it is escaped for XML, and the package contains no document type declaration and no external relationship.
- [ ] Given a construct the writer approximates, when exported, then the job's warnings name it; for a document imported from Word, the preservation layer applies instead (EXP-118), because the owner reversed assumption A2 on 2026-11-06.
- [ ] Given the shared numbering and layout fixtures, when the DOCX writer runs them, then they pass.
- [ ] Given a produced package, when CI validates it against the OOXML schemas, then it is valid.

**In progress:** lane V-EXP-081-096

**Evidence:** criterion 3: `packages/export-docx/test/writer.test.ts`, 'writes numbering whose labels, under ECMA-376, equal the shared algorithm (EXP-090)'. The numbering fixture is written, and the labels Word computes from `word/numbering.xml` and `word/document.xml` under ECMA-376 (the test's `wordLabels`) equal the model's `computeLabels`, which the test also checks against the expected labels (`I`, `1.`, `1.a)`, a dash, `II`, and a restarted list). Two lists of one definition get separate abstract numberings.

Criterion 4 (lane export-docx-2, round 6): `packages/export-docx/test/exp-090-escape.test.ts` (fast-check, seed 20261113, 40 runs; well-formed strings with control characters and markup, written as paragraph text and as a paragraph style name: every part parses, none has a document type declaration, no relationship is external, and the text read back equals `xmlSafe` of the text written). The property found a gap, now closed: `el()` in `src/writer-xml.ts` wrote attribute values, such as a style name, without the XML 1.0 rule, and `textElement` did not apply it either. `writer.test.ts` still passes (97 tests with the new file).

**Notes:** Lane w2 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing ticked, no code written. Criterion 1 and criterion 5 need a `docx` target in the export worker, and `services/export-worker` has no dependency on `@docedit/export-docx` (it lists authority, document-service, export-latex and model), so the worker cannot call `writeDocx`; adding the workspace dependency changes the package manifest and the lockfile, which this run does not do (needsDependency: `@docedit/export-docx` for `services/export-worker`). With it, the job would be a copy of the `html` branch (ADR 0371): `writeDocx` gives bytes and `warnings`, which become the job's warnings (criterion 5 for a document made in the editor; an imported document needs the original package, which the worker does not read, EXP-118). Criterion 1 also needs Word and LibreOffice, which are not installed here. Criterion 2 needs a model of how Word resolves styles, run against the 27 cases of `packages/model/fixtures/semantics/cascade.json`; the fixtures encode the model's order, and owner decision 9 (2026-11-15, `docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`) changes the order of numbering indent and paragraph-style indent to Word's, so the fixture `paragraph-style-over-numbering-level` and `resolveParagraph` are to change first (CDM-005); a Word-side test written now would pin the old order. Criterion 6 waits for EXP-115, which is not built. Criterion 7 needs the OOXML schemas, which are not in the repository, and a CI job. Earlier: Open (2026-11-13, round 4): criterion 1 needs Word and LibreOffice, and criterion 7 needs OOXML schema validation in CI; neither is run here. Criterion 2 needs the cascade of 03 §6 computed for the corpus. Criterion 4 (round 6, lane export-docx-2) is ticked for documents without hyperlinks: a hyperlink carries the one external relationship a document has by design, read as proposed in docs/adr/0144-export-docx-2-backlog-batch.md. The criterion as written forbids every external relationship; the owner or the spec should confirm that reading. Criterion 4 before round 6 forbade every external relationship, but the package check in `writer.test.ts` allows hyperlinks (SR-M18), so the wording needs the owner's or the story's decision. Criterion 5 is tested for one construct only (a floating figure). Criterion 6 needs the EXP-115 fixtures, which are not built. Criterion 7 (round 2, lane EXP-079-091): no OOXML schema validation exists in the repository (a search of `packages/export-docx`, `packages/export-latex` and `tests` finds none), so it stays open (ADR 0227 decision 3). Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported. Phase 4 follows 11 §7. The writer is the direct OOXML writer; the document's `requires` check of 03 §2 applies as for the LaTeX converter (EXP-002). Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-M18: "no external relationship" in the fourth criterion would forbid hyperlinks, which are external relationships, and an imported package keeps its own; the review proposes "no external relationship except hyperlinks, beyond those the original package had" ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)).

### EXP-091 Write DOCX tables, images and captions

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 03 §4.3, §10 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-090

As an author, I want tables, pictures and captions in the Word file, so that structured documents survive the export.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a table with spans, borders, shading, `headerRows`, banded rows and a `grid`, when exported, then the Word table has the same structure, widths and repeated header rows.
- [x] Given inline images and figures with `width`, `height`, `align` and `alt`, when exported, then the pictures appear at those sizes with alternative text.
- [x] Given a figure caption with a `seq` field, when exported, then the label shown equals the shared algorithm's.
- [x] Given a row taller than a page, when exported, then it is written as a Word row, which Word can split, and no continuation rows are made.
- [x] Given an asset, when it is included, then it is taken from the document's attached assets only (invariant I4).

**Evidence:** criterion 1: `packages/export-docx/test/writer.test.ts`, 'writes tables with their grid, spans, header rows, borders, shading and margins, and pictures as parts (EXP-091)': the grid widths, a header row, a row that cannot split, a column span, a row span with its continuing cell, shading, a double border, the table style, and banded rows (`noHBand` is 0). Criterion 2, in part: the same test checks that the picture extent of 457,200 EMU is written, and 'escapes every string' in the same file reads the alternative text back. Its `align` is not checked. Round 5, `packages/export-docx/test/exp-091-095.test.ts`: 'writes a row taller than a page as one splittable Word row, with no continuation rows (EXP-091)' (a 200-paragraph row carries no `cantSplit`; a row the model keeps together does, as the control); 'takes a picture only from the document assets, and writes an asset that is missing as its alternative text (EXP-091, I4)' (one media part, the attached bytes, a missing asset is not guessed and warns); 'writes a figure with its alignment and alternative text, and its caption number equals the shared algorithm (EXP-091)' (figure `align` gives `w:jc`, the alternative text reads back, each `SEQ Figure` result equals `computeLabels`). Round 6 (lane export-docx-2): `packages/export-docx/test/exp-091-figures.test.ts` (fast-check, seed 20261113, 30 runs over sizes of 1 to 9000 twips, every alignment value and any alternative text): a figure's extent is its size times 635 EMU, its `descr` is the alternative text (none when it is empty), and its paragraph's `w:jc` is `center`, `right` for `end`, `left` for `start`, and absent otherwise. Criterion 2 stays open: the inline-image part (`align` on an inline picture, which the model may not carry) is not checked here. Criterion 2 (lane w2, 2026-11-17): `packages/export-docx/test/exp-091-095.test.ts`, 'writes an inline picture at its size with its alternative text, and the alignment of its paragraph (EXP-091)' (an inline `image` in a centred paragraph is a `wp:inline`, never an anchor; its extent is its size in twips times 635; its `descr` is the alternative text, read back unescaped; the paragraph carries `w:jc`). Figures: the tests above.

**Notes:** Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criterion 2 is ticked on the spec's own attribute table. 03 §4.4 gives the inline `image` the attributes `asset`, `width`, `height` and `alt` only, and `align` belongs to `figure` (03 §4.3), so for an inline picture "align" is read as the alignment of its paragraph, which the writer writes. This reading is proposed, not confirmed: if the owner wants a per-image alignment on inline pictures, the model needs a new attribute first (a CDM change). The criterion's wording could be changed to "figures with `align`; inline images with `width`, `height` and `alt`". Open (2026-11-13, round 4): criterion 2 lacks the `align` check. Criterion 3 is not met: the test counts two `SEQ Figure` fields but does not compare their results with the shared algorithm's labels. Criterion 4 (a row taller than a page, written as a row Word can split, with no continuation rows) and criterion 5 (assets only from the attached set, I4) have no test. Round 5 (2026-11-13, ADR 0117): criteria 3, 4 and 5 are ticked on the tests named in the Evidence. For criterion 5, "attached" is taken as the asset store the caller passes for the document; the writer writes only the assets its nodes refer to, and which assets the job attaches is for EXP-098. Criterion 2 stays open: the figure's `align` and alternative text are now checked, and one picture's size is checked, but the `align` of an inline image is not. Round 2 (lane EXP-079-091, ADR 0227 decision 2): the CDM `image` node has no `align` attribute (`packages/model/src/definition/cdm1.ts`), so the writer cannot meet this for inline images without a model change; the reading (figures only, or a model change) is for the owner or the spec. Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported.

### EXP-092 Write DOCX page structure, notes and references

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 03 §4.1, §4.2, §4.4, §10, §7 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-090

As an author, I want page setups, headers, footers, footnotes, contents and references in the Word file, so that the document keeps its structure.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given page setups and section breaks, when exported, then sections have their size, orientation, margins and columns, and headers and footers have first-page and even-page variants as the setup says.
- [x] Given a footnote, when exported, then it is a Word footnote of one paragraph.
- [x] Given a `toc` and lists of figures or tables, bookmarks and cross-references, when exported, then they are fields that Word can update, and the labels equal the shared algorithm's.
- [x] Given `page` and `pageCount` fields, when exported, then Word fields are written; given a `date` field, then the server's date at job creation in the job's time zone is written, and never a client value.
- [x] Given tab stops, `watermark`, `pageNumberStart` and `pageNumberFormat`, when exported, then they are written as native properties.

**Evidence:** criteria 1, 4 and 5: `packages/export-docx/test/writer.test.ts` (EXP-092 test: two sections, the landscape one in two columns and starting on an odd page, first-page and even-page footers, `PAGE` and `NUMPAGES` in the header, a `DATE` field of 07/11/2026 for Pacific/Auckland at the instant the caller passes, the Roman start of page 3); `packages/export-docx/test/exp-092-094.test.ts` (each section's size and margins, the landscape size from its page setup; the `w:tabs` right stop at 2880 with a dot leader; the `DRAFT` watermark and `pgNumType` on the section). Criterion 2: `packages/export-docx/test/exp-092-094.test.ts` (the footnote's part holds one paragraph and so does the footnote node). Criterion 3 (round 4, lane export-docx-2, 2026-11-13): `packages/export-docx/test/exp-092-lists.test.ts` (the list-of-figures entries show `Figure <label>: <caption>` with the label `computeLabels` gives each caption, and the `TOC` field instruction names `"Figure"`; a cross-reference's cached text equals `referenceText` for its target). The fix is in `packages/export-docx/src/writer.ts` (`shownText`, which replaces the caption's `seq` fields by their labels in the list entries; before it they were empty).

**Notes:** Criterion 3 ticked (round 4, 2026-11-13): the list-of-figures entries had lost their caption labels (the writer's text reader ignored `seq` fields), and the test in Evidence showed it; the writer now reads each list entry with its labels. The date criterion is met by construction: the writer reads the instant from its options and never from the client; the test passes the instant. Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported.

### EXP-093 Write suggestions and comments to DOCX

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 09 §10, §11; 03 §5, §10; ADR 0015 · **Requirements:** R5, R8 · **Depends on:** EXP-090, EXP-081, SYN-032

As an owner, I want my review to travel into Word as tracked changes and comments, so that a reviewer in Word sees what I see.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given suggestions and `suggestions` is `markup`, when exported, then insertions and deletions become `w:ins` and `w:del` with an author and a date.
- [ ] Given the date of a suggestion, when written, then it is the server time of the commit that introduced the suggestion's `id`.
- [x] Given suggestions and `suggestions` is `accept` or `reject`, when exported, then the file holds no tracked changes and is as if all were accepted or rejected.
- [ ] Given comment threads at the exported `seq`, when exported, then they become Word comments anchored to their range or block, with replies.
- [x] Given a suggestion on a paragraph, a whole table row or a whole table, when exported with `markup`, then it becomes the matching tracked block change.

**Evidence:** criteria 1 and 3: `packages/export-docx/test/writer.test.ts`, 'writes suggestions as tracked changes with author and date, or settles them (EXP-093)'. Criterion 1: a `w:ins` with the author 'Ada Lovelace' and the given date, a `w:del` with its author, and a row's `w:del` with its date. Criterion 3: with `suggestions` set to `accept` and to `reject`, no `w:ins`, `w:del` or `w:rPrChange` remains, the accepted text stays, the rejected text goes, and rejected formatting returns to its old state. Criterion 5, in part: a paragraph mark is written as inserted and a row as deleted, and, for a whole table, every row carries the change (round 5: `packages/export-docx/test/exp-091-095.test.ts`, 'writes a whole-table suggestion as a tracked change on every row', with the commit dates).

**Notes:** Open (2026-11-13, round 4): criterion 2 needs the commit time of each suggestion, which the host supplies (EXP-098 is not built); the writer test passes the times in. Criterion 4 (comments as Word comments with replies) has no test. Round 5: criterion 5 is met for a whole table (see Evidence). Criterion 4 stays open: the writer has no comment part, so no Word comment or reply is written. Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported. Where Word's author names come from is open: the service stores per-tenant surrogates, not names (08 §13; 13 §5). How `fmt` suggestions are written is not specified. 08 §6 also mentions "compare, then export as DOCX with tracked changes"; 09 does not describe it and no story is written here (Open questions). Lane EXP-092-104 (2026-11-13, round 6): criterion 4 is built for block anchors only. `packages/export-docx/src/writer-comments.ts` writes each thread of `WriteOptions.comments` as Word comments on its paragraph (range start, range end and reference per message), with replies in `comments.xml` and linked to their root in `commentsExtended.xml`; `src/writer.ts` wires it in `paragraphXml` and `pack`. Tests: `packages/export-docx/test/exp-093-comments.test.ts` (8 tests, including a fast-check escaping property). Criterion 4 stays unticked because the thread target in 07 §6 is a text range as well as a block, and the export input has no offsets within a paragraph; the input shape is proposed in docs/adr/0228-lane-EXP-092-104.md (status: proposed). Whether the writer must map a range to runs is an owner or spec question. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing new ticked, no code written. Criterion 2 has two halves. The writer half is built: `WriteOptions.suggestionDates` is the only source of a `w:date`, a suggestion with no entry gets none, and no client value is read. The other half is the host passing the commit time of the commit that introduced each suggestion `id`; that is the docx export job, which does not exist (the export worker has no `docx` target and no dependency on `@docedit/export-docx`, see EXP-090 Notes), so the criterion as a whole is not met end to end. Criterion 4 needs the range anchor, which depends on an input shape that the spec leaves open (07 §6 keeps the range in the document service, and the export input of ADR 0228 carries only a block); the owner owns the choice between an offset in characters, a position in the model, or block anchors only, so none was made here.

### EXP-094 Write regions to DOCX

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 09 §11; 07 §15; 07 §11; 11 §7 · **Requirements:** R3, R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-090, PRM-018, PRM-046, gap: no story yet for the export-identities callback of 07 §15, which 05 §8.1 lacks

As an owner, I want regions to carry into the Word file as protected areas as far as the format allows, so that a Word user is guided to the parts they may edit and the file keeps the shape of my document's rights.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a region, when exported, then it becomes a block-level content control tagged with the region identifier and title.
- [x] Given at least one region that is not editable by everyone, when exported, then the file gets document protection in read-only mode and editable ranges (`w:permStart` and `w:permEnd`); given every region is editable by everyone, then no protection is added.
- [x] Given the host supplies export identities through the opt-in callback, when exported, then per-user editable ranges are written for those identities only; without it, only the `everyone` group is used.
- [x] Given an `approved` region, when exported, then it becomes a content control with locked content.
- [x] Given the grants, when exported, then the original grants are kept in a custom XML part.
- [x] Given the export result, when it is read, then the warnings state that Word's protection is advisory and that enforcement exists only inside the document service.

**Evidence:** criteria 1 to 6: `packages/export-docx/test/writer.test.ts` (EXP-094 test: the region tag `lockedRegion0001` and its title as alias, read-only protection with `permStart` ranges for `everyone` and for the named editor, `sdtContentLocked` for the approved region, the `urn:docedit:grants:1` part with the grant, the warning that protection is advisory; no protection without the callback). `packages/export-docx/test/exp-092-094.test.ts` (one body-level control per region, tagged and titled; no read-only protection and no range when every region is editable by everyone, and no advisory warning; with no export identities only the `everyone` group is written; the named identity's range is written for the restricted region alone and wraps its control; the warning says the protection is advisory and enforced only inside the document service).

**Notes:** Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported. Phase 4: 11 §7 ("the direct writer, including fields and regions"). The protection in the file is advisory, as the file format's specification says; the enforcement of 07 exists only while the document lives in the document service. Whether the export should say so to the user is open (Open questions); the last criterion takes it that it should. The opt-in callback is not in the list of callbacks in 05 §8.1. This story absorbed PRM-055 during the cross-check.

### EXP-095 Write form fields to DOCX

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Owner |

**Spec:** 12 §12, §13; 09 §11; 03 §10 · **Requirements:** R5, R13 · **Depends on:** EXP-090, FRW-002, FRW-003

As an owner, I want fields to become Word content controls, so that a form can be completed in Word and its data read back, and the file keeps its blanks, labels and values.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given a `form_field`, when exported, then it becomes an inline content control with the field's `key` as tag, its `label` as alias, a lock, and data binding to a custom XML part that holds the values.
- [x] Given assignees and rules, when exported, then they are stored in a second custom XML part, separate from the values.
- [x] Given values with XML special characters, when exported, then they are escaped in the custom part.
- [x] Given the file, when it is opened, then Word's "filling in forms" protection is set.
- [x] Given the export result, when it is read, then the warnings state that this protection is advisory and that enforcement exists only while the document lives in the document service.
- [ ] Given the custom parts, when the export is made for a requester, then they hold only the values that requester may read (ADR 0019), and the export result says which were redacted.

**Evidence:** criterion 1 (lane U-EXP-081-096, `packages/export-docx/test/exp-095-bindings.test.ts`, run with `writer.test.ts`, `exp-091-095.test.ts` and `exp-092-094.test.ts`, 112 tests pass): each of the six controls carries a `w:dataBinding` whose XPath names the field of its own key, checkbox and date fields included; checkbox values are stored as `true` or `false`, and dates as a dateTime (docs/adr/0322-lane-U-EXP-081-096.md). Criteria 3, 4 and 5 (round 4) and criterion 2 (round 5, `packages/export-docx/test/exp-091-095.test.ts`, 'keeps assignees and rules in a definitions part of their own': a `role_def` and a `rule_def` share the definitions part, and the values part holds neither): `packages/export-docx/test/writer.test.ts`, 'writes form fields as content controls bound to a custom XML part of their values (EXP-095)'. Six inline controls (each in a paragraph) carry their `key` as tag, their label as alias, a lock and a data binding to the values part. The values part holds `Ann &amp; Bob &lt;x&gt;`, which reads back as `Ann & Bob <x>`. The role definition (assignee) is in a second part, which holds no values. The settings part sets `w:edit="forms"`, and the export warns that the form protection is advisory and that enforcement is the document service's. Criterion 6 is tested only in part: `writer.test.ts`, 'writes a projection from the model alone', checks that the export reports `secret.value` as redacted, and does not read the custom parts. Round 5, `packages/export-docx/test/exp-091-095.test.ts`, 'leaves a redacted form value out of every part of a requester's package': a sentinel value held under `redacted` appears in no part of the package, the values part holds an empty field for its key, and the export names the key.

**Notes:** Criterion 1 ticked (lane U-EXP-081-096, round 9): `formFieldXml` in `packages/export-docx/src/writer.ts` had left `w:dataBinding` out for `checkbox` and `date` fields; the exclusion is removed. The binding format for those two kinds is a choice made here, not in the spec, and is proposed in docs/adr/0322-lane-U-EXP-081-096.md. Earlier audit (2026-11-13): the test read only the first binding, so it did not see two of six controls unbound. Round 5 (2026-11-13): criterion 2 is ticked (see Evidence). Criterion 6 stays open: the form-values part is checked, but the grants part (`grants`, written as the host gives it) is not checked against a requester's visibility, and the criterion covers every custom part. Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported. Phase 4 follows 12 §13 ("DOCX round trip of fields"), but 12 §12 specifies only the export side. Reading these controls back on import is not described in 09 or 12 §12 (Open questions). How a `signature` field is exported is not stated. This story absorbed FRW-082 during the cross-check. Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-M15: Word re-reads a bound content control from its custom XML part, so for an imported form the writer must treat the control and the part as one unit, and the mapping of Word's own content controls to `form_field` kinds is still undecided; 12 §12 does not yet say that custom parts are redacted per requester (SR-N47) ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)). Round 8 (2026-11-13, lane export-docx-2): criterion 6 stays open. The writer has no visibility input of its own; it sees only the redacted form keys and the hidden-region placeholders. The grants part and the role and rule definitions part are written as the host gives them, and whether a restricted requester may read them is undecided (ADR 0144, open questions). Lane w2 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing new ticked, no code written. The values part and the export's `redacted` list are tested (Evidence). What is undecided is whether a requester without `manage` may read the grants part (EXP-094) and the role and rule definitions part: ADR 0019 §5 says exports run as the requester's projection and lists EXP-095 as assuming full access, but it does not say what a projection of those two parts is (a rule can name a hidden field and state a threshold). The owner's answers of 2026-11-15 (`docs/owner-decisions-2026-11-15.md`) do not cover it. The spec's default is that the host passes the parts as it wants them seen, which is what the writer does; ticking the criterion on that default would hide the open question, so it stays open.

### EXP-096 Write math to DOCX

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | M | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §6.1; 03 §10 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-054, EXP-049, EXP-090

As an author, I want equations to stay equations in the Word file, so that they can be edited there.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given an equation that passes the gate, when exported, then the server converts it from LaTeX to Office math through MathML with the converter chosen in EXP-054.
- [ ] Given a converter licensed under the GPL, when it is used, then it runs as a separate process.
- [ ] Given an equation that cannot be converted, when exported, then it becomes an image whose alternative text is the LaTeX source.
- [ ] Given an equation that fails the gate, when exported, then it is replaced as in EXP-051.

**Evidence:** none ticked. Built for criterion 4, in part (lane w2, 2026-11-17): `packages/export-docx/test/exp-096-gate.test.ts` (a stub gate that refuses `\def`: a refused display equation and a refused inline equation are each written as `[equation not exported]` with no trace of their source, the warnings name the display's block and the inline equation's paragraph, and the other paragraphs and the accepted equation are written; with no gate nothing is checked).

**Notes:** Moved from phase 4 to phase 2 and raised to must on 2026-11-06: the owner decided that the lossless DOCX writer ships with import (ADR 0017), so Word files round-trip as soon as they can be imported. Second review (2026-11-06, SPK-027), SR-M10: for an imported equation the criteria apply only after an edit; while it is unedited, its original Office math is written back, which needs inline atoms to keep their original XML ([review](../review/2026-11-06-second-review.md)). Checked 2026-11-13 (ADR 0117, round 8): no criterion ticked. The writer writes the LaTeX source as text; criteria 1 and 2 need the converter of EXP-054 (not done); criterion 3 needs an equation renderer outside this package; criterion 4 needs the gate of EXP-049 and the replacement of EXP-051. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): partial work, nothing ticked. `WriteOptions.mathGate` in `packages/export-docx/src/writer.ts` takes the gate as a function, as `RoomInit.mathGate` does for the authority, because `@docedit/export-docx` depends on `@docedit/model` only and the gate lives in `@docedit/export-latex` (adding the dependency is a manifest and lockfile change, which this run does not make). Criterion 4 stays unticked: the gate is tested here with a stub, and no caller passes the real `mathGateReason` to the writer, because the export worker has no `docx` target (see EXP-090 Notes). Wire it with `mathGate: mathGateReason` when that target exists, and add a test with the real gate then. The placeholder text and its wording are a choice made here, proposed, not specified: 09 §6.3 names a "visible placeholder" for the PDF only. Criteria 1 to 3 need the converter of EXP-054, which is not chosen: its measurement criteria are open (no Office math sample and no converter in the repository). Criterion 3 also needs an equation renderer, which does not exist.

## DOCX import

### EXP-097 Choose the parser to build the DOCX importer on

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| spike | 2 | M | must | Maintainer |

**Spec:** 09 §12; 11 §1 (principle 3); research summary §4 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** none

As a maintainer, I want a time-boxed evaluation of existing DOCX parsers, so that the importer builds on a permissively licensed, maintained and safe base where one fits.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given the candidates (for example mammoth, which produces lossy HTML without sanitising and had CVE-2025-11849, the Open XML SDK under MIT, and others), when they are compared, then the report gives licence, maintenance state, security history and fit with the CDM.
- [ ] Given a corpus of real Word files and the hostile corpus of EXP-110, when each candidate reads them, then the report gives how much structure each recovers and how each behaves on hostile files.
- [ ] Given a candidate in another runtime than Node.js, when it is recommended, then the report states how it would run in a sandboxed worker and what that means for the reference service.
- [ ] Given the time box ends, when the report is delivered, then it recommends adopt, fork or build, with evidence.

### EXP-098 Import a Word file as a job

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §12; 05 §6.2, §7; 04 §3.2, §10; 07 §11, §12, §15; 08 §4 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-099, EXP-100, EXP-108, EXP-109, PRO-066, SYN-005, SYN-030

As an author, I want to start from my existing Word file, so that I can bring my work into the editor.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given an uploaded `.docx` asset, when the host calls `POST /documents/{id}/imports` with `asset` and `format` `docx` using its service credential, then a job is returned and `/imports/{jid}` reports its state and, when complete, its warnings.
- [x] Given the import completes, when the content is stored, then it is written as a snapshot commit with origin `import`, connected clients receive `sync.reset` with reason `import`, and a version of kind `import` is created.
- [x] Given the job ends, when it completes or fails, then `import.completed` or `import.failed` is raised and the webhooks `dep.import.completed` or `dep.import.failed` are sent.
- [x] Given a document with an `approved` region that the import would change, when the import is requested, then it is refused until the state is changed.
- [x] Given an import, when it finishes, then it creates no grants and no workflow state.
- [ ] Given `POST /documents` is called with an import, when it completes, then the new document opens with the imported content.
- [ ] Given a Word file, when it is imported, completed in fill mode and signed, then the workflow ends in a sealed PDF.

**Evidence:** criteria 1 to 5 (lane w2, 2026-11-17): `services/document-service/src/control-plane/exp-098-import-job.test.ts` (16 tests, run in memory and on PostgreSQL, real data plane: a service credential gets 202 with `Location`, `GET /imports/{jid}` reports `state` and, when completed, the warnings, a user token is refused with 403, and an asset that is missing, of another document, not docx or a format other than docx is refused; the stored content is read back, the commit is `kind` snapshot with `origin` import in `doc_commits` and the commits route answers `reset` at its `seq`, a version of kind `import` is stored at that `seq`, `dep.import.completed.v1` and `dep.import.failed.v1` are raised, the recording data plane receives `import.completed` and `import.failed`; the request is refused with `conflict` for an approved region and `workflow_active`, and a job whose region became approved after the request fails with `conflict`; the document's grants and workflow state are equal before and after) and `services/document-service/test/control-plane/exp-098-real-importer.test.ts` (the real `importDocx` on `poi/sample.docx` through the route, the job and the room; a document that lacks the `preserve` feature fails the job and stores nothing). Criterion 2 also rests on the data plane's own tests of the snapshot path (`src/data-plane/snapshot-reset.test.ts`, `src/data-plane/be-037-replace-outbox.test.ts`: `sync.reset` with reason `import` to open channels). Code: `services/document-service/src/control-plane/imports.ts`, `src/server.ts` (the `importer` option), proposed in docs/adr/0373-w2-EXP-098-import-job.md.

**Notes:** 07 §12 makes import a service-credential operation, so the host calls it on behalf of the author (naming them with `DEP-Actor`); the embedding API has no import method (Open questions). The last criterion is the phase 2 exit criterion of 11 §5. Lane U-EXP-097-112 (2026-11-13): the import route and job are still not built (`services/document-service/src/control-plane/handlers.ts` refuses `import` as a source). The PostgreSQL test `services/document-service/test/data-plane/u-exp-097-112.import-commit.pg.test.ts` shows that an imported envelope is refused unless the document lists its features: an import that keeps Word content as `preserve` atoms needs a document whose `requires` includes `preserve` (the room refuses it with `feature_missing`, `I1`). The job must decide how that feature is added (an owner or spec decision, not made here). No criterion is ticked by this lane. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): criteria 1 to 5 are ticked; see ADR 0373 for the choices made (not owner decisions). The importer is a port of `startService`: the service has no dependency on `@docedit/export-docx`, so the default `main.ts` does not offer `/imports` until a host passes the importer in or the owner allows that workspace dependency (needsDependency in the lane report). The job runs in the service process, not in the sandboxed worker of EXP-099. Feature question: the job does not add `preserve` or other features to the document; a document without them fails the job with `validation_failed` (I1). Criterion 6 stays open: an asset is registered for a document, so no asset exists before `POST /documents` creates one; an owner or spec decision is needed on how a new document gets its source file. Criterion 7 stays open: it needs the fill workflow and the seal, which are not built. Pictures and other assets the importer makes are not stored by the job (ADR 0373, item 6).

### EXP-099 Read the DOCX package safely

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| enabler | 2 | L | must | Operator |

**Spec:** 09 §12; 10 §3.5; 11 §1 · **Requirements:** R5, R10 · **Depends on:** EXP-097, BE-043

As an operator, I want the package read in a sandbox with hard limits, so that a hostile Word file cannot harm the service.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [ ] Given a file, when the importer runs, then it runs in a sandboxed worker with no network and no credentials.
- [x] Given limits on entry count, total uncompressed size and compression ratio, when a file exceeds any of them, then it is rejected before it is fully expanded.
- [x] Given any XML part with a document type declaration, when the importer reads it, then the file is rejected.
- [x] Given a relationship with an external target (remote template, linked image or hyperlink to a file), when the importer reads it, then it is never followed and no network request is made.
- [x] Given a macro-enabled file, when it is uploaded, then it is refused with a clear message.
- [x] Given an embedded object, when the importer meets it, then it treats it as opaque: it never parses or runs it, and keeps it only as a preserved fragment (EXP-117).

**Evidence:** criterion 2: `packages/export-docx/test/exp-099-limits.test.ts` (round 4, lane V-EXP-097-112: `importDocx` on `poi/sample.docx` is refused with the entry limit, the total size limit and the ratio limit, and accepted under the default limits; the entry check runs on the central directory, before any entry is inflated, and the size and ratio checks are the `readZip` ones), with `packages/export-docx/test/zip-bounded.test.ts` (a header that declares a small size over an entry that inflates to more is refused during inflate, not after it is fully expanded; `src/zip.ts` `inflateBounded`) and `test/layers.test.ts` (entry count, total size and ratio). Criterion 6: `packages/export-docx/test/embedded-opaque.test.ts` (the embedding part of `poi/EmbeddedDocument.docx` is kept byte for byte under `parts/` in the atom's fragment). Criteria 3 to 5: `packages/export-docx/test/package-safety.test.ts` (a document type declaration is refused in the document, styles and settings parts; an external relationship is kept as data, and a stubbed `fetch` is never called while the fixture imports; a macro-enabled main part is refused with "macro-enabled files are refused"); `packages/export-docx/test/layers.test.ts` (the same refusals at the parser and the package level). Criterion 2 has the limit checks in `test/layers.test.ts`, but see the Notes.

**Notes:** The values of the three limits are not given in 09 (Open questions). Open (2026-11-13): criterion 1 needs the sandboxed worker of BE-043, not built here. Criterion 2 (2026-11-13, second round): the entry count, total size and ratio still use the sizes the central directory declares, but `entryData` now inflates through fflate's streaming `Inflate` in 64 KiB slices and refuses the entry as soon as its output passes the declared size, so a header that lies is stopped during inflate. The ratio check is still judged on declared sizes only. Criterion 6 is ticked on the fragment's byte-for-byte copy of the embedding; the fragment's other parts remain EXP-117's. Lane U-EXP-097-112 (round 4, 2026-11-13): criterion 1 stays open. `importDocx` runs in the calling Node process; the asset worker's Podman sandbox (`services/asset-worker/src/sandbox-podman.ts`) takes images and SVG only, so a DOCX job kind, an image with the package and a change in `services/asset-worker` are needed first (see docs/adr/0323-lane-U-EXP-097-112.md). Evidence audit (2026-11-13): criterion 2 is unticked because no test exceeds the entry-count limit (`test/layers.test.ts` passes `maxEntries: 10` over an archive of one entry, so only the size and ratio limits are exercised, and the Evidence line wrongly says entry count is covered), and no test drives the limits through `importDocx`; add a test that reads an archive with more entries than `maxEntries`, then re-tick. Done (lane V-EXP-097-112, round 4): `test/exp-099-limits.test.ts` drives the entry, size and ratio limits through `importDocx`; criterion 2 is ticked. Criterion 1 (the sandboxed worker) stays open, as in the note above. Lane w2 (2026-11-17): blocked, nothing new ticked, no code written. The asset worker now validates a `.docx` upload in its sandbox (`services/asset-worker/src/sandbox/zip.ts`, `processDocx`: a check of the package, not the import), but nothing runs `importDocx` there, and the criterion asks for the importer itself to run in a worker with no network and no credentials. That is a DOCX import job kind, a sandbox image with the package, and a run under Podman that shows the network is closed; it belongs to the import job of EXP-098 and is not built here. A mock of the sandbox would not meet the criterion.

### EXP-100 Import text, styles and numbering

| Type | Phase | Size | Priority | Persona |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| story | 2 | L | must | Author |

**Spec:** 09 §12; 03 §4.1, §4.3, §4.5, §9 · **Requirements:** R5 · **Depends on:** EXP-099, EXP-097

As an author, I want the text, styles and lists of my Word file to arrive as styles and lists, so that I can keep editing with the same structure.

**Acceptance criteria**

- [x] Given Word paragraphs and runs, when imported, then they become paragraphs, text and marks of the CDM, and formatting that has no CDM property is reported as a warning.
- [x] Given Word styles with inheritance, when imported, then they become `style_def` nodes with the `basedOn` chain kept, and built-in headings map to the reserved identifiers `Heading1` to `Heading9`.
- [ ] Given Word numbering, when imported, then it becomes `numbering_def` nodes and paragraph `numbering` with `listId`, and the labels the editor computes equal those Word displays for the corpus.
- [x] Given imported definitions, when they are stored, then they get fresh random identifiers, never identifiers taken from the file.
- [x] Given the document defaults of the file, when imported, then they become `settings` and the default paragraph style.
